Table 1
Q&A NGO Accounting
1. Q: Why should a Finance Manager treat day-to-day finance operations as one controlled process rather than separate bookkeeping activities?
A: Show answer
In a not-for-profit environment, accounts payable, accounts receivable, purchasing, corporate credit cards, bank reconciliations, grants, and reporting are connected. The Finance Manager should establish clear ownership, cut-off dates, approval paths, coding rules, and reconciliation procedures across the cycle. For GWAHS, the Team Leader–Finance role specifically brings these operational areas together while also requiring timely and compliant reporting. For restricted research grants, the same principle applies because project expenditure, donor claims, receivables, partner balances, and reporting deadlines depend on reliable underlying transactions. A practical control framework should define who initiates, reviews, approves, posts, reconciles, and closes each transaction type. Power Query can consolidate AP, AR, bank, purchasing, and grant extracts into one management view and identify missing or unmatched records. VBA can generate daily exception lists and month-end task trackers. The Finance Manager should still retain human review over unusual transactions, because automation should improve consistency rather than replace financial judgment.

Send to the team:
- "Please treat AP, AR, purchasing, cards, bank and grant transactions as one controlled finance cycle with clear ownership and reconciliation points."
Key point:
- Integrated finance processes reduce gaps between transaction processing and reporting.
- Automation should surface exceptions while management retains control over approvals and judgment.
Official links:
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
2. Q: How would you structure the accounts payable and purchasing control environment in a funded not-for-profit organization?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should ensure that a supplier invoice can be traced from purchase request or procurement evidence through approval, receipt of goods or services, accounting entry, payment authorization, and bank settlement. The control should confirm that the expenditure is allowable, correctly coded, within delegated authority, and consistent with the relevant grant or program budget where funding restrictions apply. GWAHS specifically places accounts payable and purchasing within the Finance team's responsibilities and also requires compliance with procurement requirements and financial delegations. For restricted grants, expenditure should additionally be checked against donor conditions and project timelines. A practical control register can include supplier, purchase reference, project, account, budget line, approver, invoice date, due date, payment batch, and supporting-document status. Power Query can combine procurement and AP files to identify invoices without purchase references or approvals. VBA can prepare an exception report for missing evidence and overdue approvals. The Finance Manager should review exception trends rather than merely processing the volume of invoices.

Send to the team:
- "Please confirm procurement evidence, delegated approval, coding, budget availability, and supporting documents before payment."
Key point:
- Accounts payable is both an accounting process and a procurement control.
- Grant-funded purchases require an additional eligibility and budget check.
Official links:
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
3. Q: Why should accounts receivable be monitored as actively as accounts payable?
A: Show answer
Receivables represent funding or other amounts expected to be collected, so an accurate ledger is necessary for both liquidity management and grant accountability. A Finance Manager should distinguish between amounts invoiced, formally approved, submitted to a donor, due under an agreement, disputed, and actually collected. In the CIFOR-ICRAF grants role, donor receivables are specifically monitored and collection is followed up, while monthly summaries identify significant donor receivables and payables. The underlying schedule should show grant, donor, claim or invoice reference, amount, due date, collection status, responsible project lead, and any unresolved query. Management should review aging and investigate old items rather than carrying them indefinitely. Power Query can compare receivable schedules with bank receipts and donor-reporting records. VBA can produce an aging dashboard and create follow-up lists. The Finance Manager should also distinguish slow collection caused by administrative delays from amounts that are genuinely disputed or unsupported, because the response differs in each case.

Send to the team:
- "Please maintain an aging schedule for receivables and record the reason, owner, and next action for every overdue balance."
Key point:
- Receivables affect both cash planning and financial reporting.
- Aging is useful only when every overdue item has a documented action.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
4. Q: How should a Finance Manager approach corporate credit-card controls?
A: Show answer
A corporate card is an efficient purchasing mechanism, but it can create weak documentation and delayed coding if controls are unclear. The Finance Manager should define cardholder limits, approved spending categories, transaction review, receipt requirements, accounting cut-off, reconciliation to the card statement, and escalation for unsupported items. GWAHS specifically includes corporate credit cards within day-to-day finance operations and separately requires compliance with financial delegations and organizational policies. Every transaction should therefore have enough evidence to demonstrate business purpose, correct coding, approval, and settlement. The Finance Manager should review unusual merchant descriptions, duplicate transactions, missing receipts, personal-looking purchases, and transactions outside delegated limits. Power Query can normalize card statements and match them to expense records, while VBA can produce missing-receipt and unresolved-item reports. The control objective is not merely to reconcile the statement but to prove that expenditure was authorized, appropriate, and correctly classified.

Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile every corporate-card statement to approved supporting documents and escalate missing or unusual transactions promptly."
Key point:
- Card reconciliation must cover both arithmetic accuracy and authorization.
- Exception reporting helps management focus on unsupported or unusual activity.
Official links:
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
5. Q: Why are bank reconciliations a management control rather than a routine clerical exercise?
A: Show answer
The bank is an independent source of cash information, while the accounting ledger represents the organization's internal record. A Finance Manager should use the reconciliation to identify unrecorded receipts, unpresented payments, bank charges, timing differences, duplicate postings, and unexplained movements. GWAHS explicitly assigns bank reconciliations to the finance function, so the control should have a documented monthly timetable, preparer and reviewer separation where practical, and evidence that reconciling items are cleared. A reconciliation that merely proves the closing balances agree is insufficient if old reconciling items remain unresolved. Power Query can standardize bank exports and compare them with the cashbook using reference, amount, date, and transaction type. VBA can prepare an exception sheet showing unmatched transactions and the age of each item. The Finance Manager should review unusual cash movements and ensure that significant unresolved items are escalated before reporting is finalized.

Send to the team:
- "Please complete bank reconciliations to a defined timetable and clear or escalate all aged reconciling items before close."
Key point:
- A bank reconciliation is an independent check on the cash ledger.
- The quality of the investigation into exceptions matters as much as the reconciliation itself.
Official links:
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
6. Q: Why should month-end close combine journals, accruals, reconciliations, and reporting rather than treat them as separate tasks?
A: Show answer
Month-end reporting is only reliable when the underlying ledger is complete and supported. A Finance Manager should establish a close calendar covering transaction cut-off, bank reconciliation, receivables, payables, accruals, prepaid items where relevant, grant coding, balance-sheet reconciliations, journal review, and final management reporting. GWAHS specifically identifies journals, accruals, reconciliations, and financial reporting as part of the month-end process. For CIFOR-ICRAF restricted grants, monthly summaries also need to reflect multi-year budgets, cumulative expenditure, donor receivables and payables, and staff time allocation. A close pack should therefore include evidence of completed reconciliations and explanations for significant variances. Power Query can refresh recurring source data and compare closing schedules, while VBA can build a close checklist, mark completed controls, and produce an exception report. Management should approve the final close only after material unexplained differences have been investigated.

Send to the team:
- "Please use one month-end close checklist covering journals, accruals, reconciliations, grants and reporting, with every exception assigned an owner."
Key point:
- Month-end is a controlled reporting cycle, not simply a journal-posting exercise.
- A visible close checklist strengthens accountability and review.
Official links:
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
7. Q: Why must accruals be based on the period to which costs relate rather than the payment date?
A: Show answer
The close process should reflect the financial activity of the reporting period even where an invoice or payment arrives later. A Finance Manager should identify services received before period end, confirm the basis for estimation, document the supporting evidence, and reverse or settle the accrual in a controlled manner in the following period. In grant-funded programs, this is particularly important where donor reports depend on a defined reporting window. An unsupported accrual can create the opposite problem by overstating expenditure or charging a grant before the cost is sufficiently evidenced. The control should therefore include the reason for the accrual, calculation basis, project or grant code, responsible budget holder, supporting evidence, and review date. Power Query can compare unmatched purchase, service, and invoice records to identify likely accrual candidates. VBA can generate the accrual schedule and reversal checklist, while leaving final approval to finance management.

Send to the team:
- "Please document the service period, calculation basis, funding code, and review date for every material accrual."
Key point:
- Accruals improve period accuracy but require evidence and review.
- Grant charging should follow both accounting timing and donor eligibility requirements.
Official links:
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
8. Q: How should annual budgeting differ from rolling budget management?
A: Show answer
An annual budget provides an approved financial plan, while a rolling budget updates the forward view as circumstances change. The Finance Manager should preserve the distinction between the approved baseline and later management revisions so that actual performance can still be compared to an understandable reference point. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires annual and rolling budget updates, and its grants responsibilities include reviewing annual grant budgets, project amendments, staff time allocation, partnerships, travel, supplies, consultants, publications, and other costs according to available restricted funds. GWAHS also requires annual budgeting and budget monitoring across grants and programs. A sound process records the original budget, approved amendments, current forecast, and remaining funding. Power Query can consolidate approved budget versions and actual results, while VBA can generate variance summaries and change logs. Management should review whether a revised budget reflects genuine operational change or simply masks unfavorable performance.

Send to the team:
- "Please retain the approved budget, every authorized revision, and the current forecast so actual performance remains traceable."
Key point:
- Rolling forecasts should update expectations without destroying budget accountability.
- Budget revisions require evidence, authorization, and a clear audit trail.
Official links:
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
9. Q: Why should budget monitoring consider both spending level and project timing?
A: Show answer
A project can be under budget and still be financially problematic if activities are delayed, or it can appear heavily spent because implementation is temporarily front-loaded. CIFOR-ICRAF requires budgets to be monitored against approved amounts, project activities, timelines, and intended utilization at project end. The Finance Manager should therefore review actual expenditure against budget, cumulative expenditure, remaining budget, implementation progress, and the time remaining on the grant. A good monthly review explains significant underspend, overspend, or unusual spending patterns and identifies whether corrective action is required. For GWAHS programs, the same discipline helps budget holders understand whether available resources are aligned with delivery plans. Power Query can compare budget, actual, cumulative, and calendar-period data, while VBA can flag unusual variance thresholds. Management should discuss material exceptions with the responsible program lead instead of relying only on percentage calculations.

Send to the team:
- "Please review budget variance together with project activity and timing so financial performance is interpreted in operational context."
Key point:
- Budget monitoring should connect money to implementation progress.
- Underspending can be as important to investigate as overspending.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
10. Q: How should a Finance Manager review restricted grant expenditure for eligibility?
A: Show answer
Restricted funding creates a second control question beyond normal accounting: whether the expenditure is permitted under the specific grant agreement. CIFOR-ICRAF requires review and verification of restricted-project expenditure against budgetary and donor requirements, and its role requires that expenditures be eligible and accepted by the donor. The Finance Manager should therefore maintain an agreement summary showing eligible cost categories, budget lines, reporting periods, special conditions, approval requirements, and supporting-document standards. Each material exception should be documented and resolved before donor reporting. For GWAHS, grant financial management similarly requires compliance with funding agreements, financial acquittals, and procurement requirements. Power Query can compare transaction-level expenditure to mapped grant rules and budget lines. VBA can generate an eligibility exception report for finance review. Automation can identify potential issues, but interpretation of donor wording should remain a controlled human decision supported by the agreement file.

Send to the team:
- "Please check restricted expenditure against both the approved budget and the applicable grant conditions before it is included in donor reporting."
Key point:
- A correctly posted transaction can still be ineligible under a grant.
- The grant agreement should be the primary reference for restricted-cost decisions.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
11. Q: Why should grant agreements be reviewed before financial implementation starts?
A: Show answer
The financial section of a grant agreement determines many downstream controls: budget structure, allowable expenditure, payment terms, reporting frequency, amendment requirements, audit expectations, partner obligations, and evidence standards. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires review of new grant agreements and amendments, while GWAHS requires compliance with funding agreements and acquittal requirements. The Finance Manager or grants finance lead should prepare an agreement summary and translate its provisions into finance-system codes, reporting calendars, responsibility assignments, and monitoring controls. Important obligations should be acknowledged by finance and communicated to project leaders. Power Query can maintain a grant master register containing start and end dates, reporting dates, budget status, and coding references. VBA can generate reminders before reporting and payment deadlines. This front-end review is more efficient than discovering a restrictive clause after expenditure has already occurred.

Send to the team:
- "Please complete a documented financial review of every new grant agreement and amendment before project finance processing begins."
Key point:
- Grant agreements define the control environment for restricted funding.
- Early interpretation reduces later eligibility disputes and reporting corrections.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
12. Q: How would you control grant budget amendments on an ongoing project?
A: Show answer
A revised grant budget should never be treated as an informal spreadsheet adjustment. The Finance Manager should establish whether the amendment is permitted, identify the approved authority, preserve the previous budget, document the reason for change, update the current approved version, and ensure the finance and grant-management systems agree. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically identifies project amendment budget preparation and revised budgets for ongoing projects as a key responsibility. The process also requires checking whether budget lines remain compliant with donor rules and whether full overhead recovery is preserved where applicable. A budget-change log should capture original amount, revised amount, variance, approval reference, effective date, and affected donor-reporting periods. Power Query can compare budget versions and highlight changed lines, while VBA can produce amendment schedules for review. This creates a stronger audit trail and reduces the risk of reporting from an obsolete budget version.

Send to the team:
- "Please retain every approved grant budget version and document the authorization and reason for each amendment."
Key point:
- Grant budget amendments are controlled changes, not ordinary spreadsheet edits.
- Version history is essential for donor reporting and audit support.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
13. Q: Why should donor financial reports be prepared from reconciled underlying records rather than manually assembled figures?
A: Show answer
Donor reports are external accountability documents, so every reported amount should be traceable to the accounting system, approved budget, supporting schedules, and grant agreement. CIFOR-ICRAF requires reliable, accurate, timely donor reports and review of Country Office reports before approval. GWAHS similarly requires funding submissions, acquittals, and financial reporting under funding agreements. The Finance Manager should establish a reporting file that links the report to the trial balance or system extract, budget, adjustments, prior reporting periods, supporting schedules, and explanation of material variances. Power Query can consolidate ledger and project data into a consistent reporting model, reducing manual re-keying. VBA can create standardized reporting packs and submission checklists. The final reviewer should verify completeness, cut-off, eligibility, arithmetic, approved budget, and reporting-period consistency before submission. Donor reporting should be evidence-based and reproducible.

Send to the team:
- "Please build donor reports from reconciled system data and retain a clear bridge from the ledger to the submitted report."
Key point:
- Donor reports should be traceable from system records to final submission.
- Reproducibility is an important control over externally reported figures.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
14. Q: How should draft and final donor financial reports be controlled?
A: Show answer
A controlled reporting process should distinguish working calculations, draft reports, reviewed versions, approved submissions, and evidence of final transmission. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires draft and final financial reports to be submitted in the grants management system and requires review of Country Office donor reports before forwarding for approval. The Finance Manager should establish version naming, reviewer sign-off, report date, submission deadline, and archive location. Any post-review adjustment should be visible and documented rather than silently replacing the prior version. Power Query can refresh the underlying model without overwriting the archived report, while VBA can create a final reporting package and log the generated file name and reporting period. A final report should be locked or archived once approved, with the supporting reconciliation retained. This makes later donor questions or audit testing much easier because the organization can reproduce what was actually submitted.

Send to the team:
- "Please separate working, draft, approved, and submitted grant reports and retain evidence of the final submission version."
Key point:
- Version control is part of donor reporting governance.
- The organization should be able to reproduce the exact report submitted.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
15. Q: Why should donor payment requests follow the contractual payment schedule?
A: Show answer
A donor payment request is not simply a request for cash; it is a contractual step linked to agreed payment terms and project conditions. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires preparation of invoice or payment requests according to the payment term and schedule in the agreement. The Finance Manager should therefore maintain a grant payment calendar showing milestone, required report or evidence, amount, due date, submission status, and receipt status. The request should be reconciled to the approved grant and current financial position before submission. Power Query can compare expected payment milestones with submitted requests and bank receipts, while VBA can produce upcoming-due and overdue lists. This also supports cash-flow forecasting because planned donor receipts should be distinguished from amounts merely expected informally. Where a payment is delayed, the underlying reason should be documented so management can distinguish operational delay from donor-query delay.

Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile each donor payment request to the agreement schedule and retain evidence of the milestone or reporting condition supporting the request."
Key point:
- Contractual payment terms should drive donor cash requests.
- A payment calendar improves both compliance and cash planning.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
16. Q: How should a Finance Manager respond to donor queries after a financial report is submitted?
A: Show answer
Donor queries should be treated as controlled follow-up items, not informal email exchanges that disappear after the response is sent. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires follow-up on donor queries or reviews of submitted financial reports. The Finance Manager should log the question, reporting period, affected amount, evidence requested, response owner, response date, and final resolution. If the query results in a financial correction, the adjustment should follow normal accounting and reporting controls and be traceable to the final outcome. Power Query can maintain a donor-query register linked to grant reports, while VBA can create overdue-response reminders. The finance team should also identify recurring query themes, because repeated questions may indicate weak report design, coding problems, inadequate documentation, or misunderstood grant conditions. The management value is therefore not only closing the individual query but reducing the probability that the same problem appears again.

Send to the team:
- "Please record every donor query with an owner, evidence requirement, response deadline, and final resolution."
Key point:
- Donor queries should be managed as controlled financial issues.
- Recurring queries can reveal weaknesses in the underlying finance process.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
17. Q: Why should Country Office donor reports receive a finance review before approval?
A: Show answer
A Country Office may prepare the operational report, but the central or assigned grants finance function still needs to assess financial accuracy, consistency with grant conditions, coding, budget utilization, and supporting evidence before the report is forwarded. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically assigns review of donor reports from assigned Country Offices before forwarding them for approval. A finance review should compare reported totals to the accounting system, budget, cumulative expenditure, prior reports, and required supporting schedules. It should also test unusual movements and investigate late or corrected transactions. Power Query can combine Country Office extracts into one review model and compare reporting totals to system data. VBA can generate standardized review checklists and record reviewer clearance. The reviewer should document any corrections made and ensure that the final report reflects the approved information rather than simply accepting the Country Office submission as received.

Send to the team:
- "Please complete a documented finance review of every Country Office donor report before forwarding it for approval."
Key point:
- Review should validate the financial data independently of the preparer's submission.
- Country reporting quality depends on consistent central controls and documented corrections.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
18. Q: How should grant budgets incorporate staff time allocation?
A: Show answer
Staff time can be a material project cost, so the Finance Manager should ensure that budget assumptions, approved allocations, timesheets where required, and actual charging remain aligned. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires collaboration with Principal Investigators on staff time allocation in the OCS planner, monthly staff time updates, and collection of timesheets where those are mandatory supporting documents. The control should identify the staff member, project, approved allocation, reporting period, timesheet status, and any adjustment. Unexplained changes can affect donor eligibility and project forecasts. Power Query can consolidate time-allocation data and identify missing or unusual records, while VBA can produce reminders for incomplete timesheets and compare planned versus recorded allocations. The Finance Manager should avoid treating time data as merely an HR issue when it affects grant cost recovery and financial reporting. Evidence should be retained in a way that links staff cost to the applicable project and reporting period.

Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile staff time allocations and required timesheets to the approved project budget before reporting grant expenditure."
Key point:
- Staff time is both a project-budget issue and a supporting-document control.
- Time allocations should be reviewed whenever project funding or activities change.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
19. Q: Why should grant budgets be tested for full overhead recovery where applicable?
A: Show answer
Direct project costs do not always represent the full economic cost of delivering a grant. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires the grants function to ensure that grants recover full overheads according to the relevant cost structure. The Finance Manager should therefore understand the approved overhead methodology, confirm the correct rate or structure, and test whether proposal and amendment budgets include the necessary recovery. The review should identify exceptions before a grant is finalized because an omission can create a structural funding shortfall that becomes difficult to correct later. Power Query can compare proposal budgets against approved cost structures and flag projects without expected overhead components. VBA can create a budget review checklist and calculate recovery summaries from standardized inputs. The objective is not to add unsupported charges but to ensure that the approved financial model reflects the organization's applicable cost structure and the donor's permitted treatment.

Send to the team:
- "Please verify the applicable overhead structure during proposal and amendment-budget review and document any donor limitation."
Key point:
- Overhead recovery should follow the approved cost structure and donor rules.
- Budget review is the right point to identify structural under-recovery.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
20. Q: How should a Finance Manager review partner financial reports under a restricted grant?
A: Show answer
Partner reporting should be assessed against the partner agreement, approved budget, donor requirements, and the organization's own financial procedures. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires monitoring of restricted-grant partners, review of partner agreements and budgets with the Program Management Unit, analysis of partner expenses, review of partner financial reports, monitoring of balances, and collection of unused funds. The Finance Manager should compare opening balance, funds received, reported expenditure, budget by line, outstanding balance, and unsupported or unusual transactions. Significant over- or underspending should be explained to project leaders and managers. Power Query can standardize partner files that arrive in different layouts and map them to a common budget structure. VBA can generate partner review checklists and flag missing reports, unsupported balances, or variances. The final review should distinguish a reporting-format issue from a genuine compliance problem and document the required follow-up.

Send to the team:
- "Please review each partner report against the signed agreement, approved budget, supporting evidence, and closing balance before accepting the expenditure."
Key point:
- Partner reporting is both an accounting and grant-compliance control.
- Standardized review allows different partner formats to be assessed consistently.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
21. Q: Why must unused partner funds be tracked until they are properly returned or otherwise settled?
A: Show answer
A partner's remaining balance is not automatically income or closed simply because the partner has submitted a report. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires monitoring partner balances and collection of unused funds. The Finance Manager should therefore maintain a partner-advance or grant-partner balance schedule showing funds transferred, expenditure accepted, expenditure pending review, and unused amounts requiring recovery or other treatment under the agreement. The schedule should be reconciled to the general ledger and partner financial reports. Power Query can combine partner submissions and transfer records, while VBA can generate recovery statements and aging reports. Management should escalate balances that remain unresolved after the reporting deadline because old partner balances can become a financial-risk issue. The control should also ensure that final grant closeout does not occur while material partner balances remain unexplained.

Send to the team:
- "Please keep partner balances open until accepted expenditure and any unused funds are fully reconciled and settled."
Key point:
- Partner balances require formal settlement, not just receipt of a report.
- Unused funds should remain visible until resolved under the agreement.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
22. Q: How should restricted grants be monitored for significant over- and under-spending?
A: Show answer
Variance analysis should go beyond identifying that a line differs from budget. CIFOR-ICRAF requires analysis of restricted-grant partner expenses and feedback to project leaders and managers on expenditure trends and significant over- or under-spending. The Finance Manager should identify material variances, determine whether they arise from timing, activity changes, reclassification, procurement delays, staff changes, or genuine budget pressure, and then document the management response. For GWAHS programs, similar budget-monitoring discipline supports financial management with Program Leaders and Managers. Power Query can calculate absolute and percentage variances and summarize trends by project and budget line, while VBA can prepare recurring exception reports. Management reporting should present both the number and the operational explanation so that decisions can be taken before the variance becomes a grant-compliance problem.

Send to the team:
- "Please explain significant grant variances by cause, impact, and corrective action rather than reporting variance percentages alone."
Key point:
- Variance analysis is most useful when it leads to a management response.
- Timing differences and structural budget pressures should not be treated as the same issue.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
- Team Leader - Finance — GWAHS
23. Q: Why should project start-up and closure be treated as controlled financial events?
A: Show answer
A project should enter the finance system with the correct agreement, budget, codes, dates, responsibility, and supporting documentation, and should not simply be left open after operational activity has ended. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires liaison with the Program Management Unit on Letter of Agreement development, project start-ups and set-up in myTree and OCS, budget management, project tracking, and closures. The Finance Manager should define start-up controls for coding, budget version, payment terms, partner details, reporting dates, and system activation. At closure, the team should reconcile expenditure, donor receivables and payables, partner balances, outstanding reports, unused funds, and any final audit obligations. Power Query can create a project status register from system extracts, while VBA can generate start-up and closeout checklists. This ensures that project closure is based on evidence rather than on an administrative end date alone.

Send to the team:
- "Please complete the project start-up and closure checklists before opening a new grant and before confirming final financial closure."
Key point:
- Project start-up creates the financial control framework.
- Closure should resolve all significant financial balances and reporting obligations.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
24. Q: How should monthly multi-year grant reporting be structured?
A: Show answer
A multi-year project requires both period movement and cumulative visibility. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires monthly summaries of restricted projects with multi-year budgets and cumulative expenses. The Finance Manager should therefore present the approved multi-year budget, current-year budget, period actuals, cumulative actuals, remaining budget, forecast or expected completion position, and major variances. This avoids the common problem of looking only at the current month while losing sight of the project's total financial position. Power Query can maintain a monthly project ledger and roll cumulative expenditure automatically, while VBA can generate standardized summary packs. Every monthly summary should reconcile to the accounting records and preserve the reporting logic used in prior periods. Management can then distinguish a normal timing variance from a project that is systematically under-spending or approaching a budget limit.

Send to the team:
- "Please report multi-year grants using both current-period and cumulative budget-versus-actual views."
Key point:
- Multi-year grants require cumulative control as well as monthly monitoring.
- Consistent monthly reporting improves trend analysis and donor forecasting.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
25. Q: Why should donor receivables and payables be monitored together?
A: Show answer
A grants finance function needs to understand both amounts expected from donors and amounts that may need to be paid or settled under project arrangements. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically assigns monitoring of donor receivables and payables, including review of monthly summaries of significant balances and explanations for items above USD 50,000. The Finance Manager should maintain separate control schedules while also reviewing the overall grant cash position. Each significant balance should have a documented cause, expected settlement date, owner, and supporting agreement or transaction reference. Power Query can consolidate receivable and payable data from accounting and treasury sources and identify balances exceeding management thresholds. VBA can prepare an exception report for significant or aged balances. The important control is that a large balance should trigger investigation rather than simply appear as a number in the monthly report.

Send to the team:
- "Please document the cause, owner, expected settlement date, and support for every significant donor receivable or payable."
Key point:
- Significant donor balances require explanation and action.
- Joint review of receivables and payables supports better cash and grant management.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
26. Q: How should incoming restricted donor funds be identified and coded?
A: Show answer
Cash receipts from restricted donors need to be associated with the correct grant, project, and financial dimensions before the amount is reported or allocated. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires identification of incoming funds from restricted donors and provision of the code to the Manager of Treasury and Fixed Assets. The Finance Manager should establish a receipt-identification process using donor reference, amount, bank date, grant agreement, project code, and expected funding schedule. Unidentified receipts should remain on an exception list until sufficient evidence is obtained. Power Query can compare bank receipts to expected donor funding schedules and highlight unmatched items. VBA can prepare a daily allocation queue and receipt-identification log. This reduces the risk that restricted cash is incorrectly associated with another grant or treated as unrestricted funding. The coding decision should be supported by documentary evidence and reviewed according to delegated authority.

Send to the team:
- "Please identify every restricted donor receipt against the correct grant and document the coding evidence before allocation."
Key point:
- Cash identification is a grant-control issue, not merely a bank-reconciliation task.
- Unidentified restricted receipts should be visible until resolved.
Official links:
- Finance Officer – Grants — CIFOR-ICRAF
27. Q: Why should funding submissions and acquittals be managed as formal financial deliverables?
A: Show answer
A funding submission establishes the financial basis on which support is requested, while an acquittal demonstrates how funds were used. GWAHS explicitly requires support for funding submissions, acquittals, and financial reporting requirements. The Finance Manager should therefore maintain a funding calendar with submission dates, financial schedules, required evidence, responsible manager, approval status, and final submission record. Before an acquittal is signed off, the reported expenditure should be reconciled to the accounting system and checked against the applicable funding agreement and budget. Power Query can prepare recurring expenditure schedules from system data, while VBA can build submission checklists and flag missing support. The same process should distinguish draft management calculations from formally approved figures. A strong acquittal process protects the organization from avoidable funding disputes and provides management with a reliable view of the status of each funded program.

Send to the team:
- "Please maintain a funding deliverables calendar and reconcile every acquittal to the accounting records before submission."
Key point:
- Acquittals are formal accountability outputs, not informal summaries.
- Submission quality depends on reconciled evidence and clear ownership of deadlines.

28. Q: How should ATO obligations be incorporated into the finance close process?
A: Show answer
Where the role has responsibility for ATO obligations, compliance should be managed through a calendar, reconciliations, evidence, and review rather than by relying on a filing deadline alone. GWAHS specifically requires compliance with ATO obligations. The Finance Manager should identify the relevant obligations applicable to the organization, reconcile underlying ledger balances to the relevant reports, confirm that required returns or payments have been reviewed and authorized, and retain submission evidence. Any difference between accounting balances and the relevant statutory calculation should be investigated before filing. Power Query can consolidate tax-related ledger information into a review schedule, while VBA can prepare a statutory-compliance checklist and flag upcoming deadlines. The precise treatment depends on the organization's circumstances and applicable Australian requirements, so the finance team should work from current internal policy and authorized tax guidance rather than embedding assumptions into an automated workbook.

Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile statutory tax obligations to the accounting records and retain evidence of review, authorization, submission, and settlement."
Key point:
- ATO compliance needs a documented reconciliation and filing control.
- Automation should support, not replace, current tax-policy review.

29. Q: Why should financial delegations be embedded into transaction controls?
A: Show answer
A delegation framework defines who can approve expenditure and under what limits. GWAHS specifically requires compliance with financial delegations as part of its finance role. The Finance Manager should ensure that purchasing, payment, journal, card, and other approval workflows reference the current delegated-authority structure. A transaction should not be considered compliant merely because the accounting code is correct if it was approved by an unauthorized person. The control should also address changes in personnel, temporary acting arrangements, and separation between preparation and approval where required. Power Query can compare transaction approvers against a controlled delegation list and flag exceptions. VBA can generate an approval-exception report for management review. The delegation register itself should be version-controlled and protected from casual editing because an outdated approval matrix can undermine otherwise accurate transaction processing.

Send to the team:
- "Please verify the current delegated authority before approving purchasing, payments, journals, and card transactions."
Key point:
- Financial accuracy does not compensate for unauthorized approval.
- Delegation registers require version control and regular review.

30. Q: How should a Finance Manager improve internal controls without making the finance process unnecessarily slow?
A: Show answer
Control improvement should focus on points where errors, non-compliance, or financial leakage can occur. GWAHS explicitly assigns responsibility for reviewing and improving finance systems, processes, workflows, and internal controls, while CIFOR-ICRAF requires strong internal controls under organizational policies and procedures. The Finance Manager should map the process, identify failure points, define preventive and detective controls, remove duplicate manual steps where safe, and measure whether the revised process actually reduces exceptions. Power Query is useful for repeated data consolidation and exception detection; VBA can automate repetitive checklists, logs, and outputs. Controls should not be added simply because they look formal. Each control should have a clear purpose, owner, frequency, evidence, and escalation path. Periodic review can then identify controls that are no longer effective or that create unnecessary administrative work without reducing meaningful risk.

Send to the team:
- "Please document the purpose, owner, frequency, evidence, and escalation point for every key finance control."
Key point:
- Good process improvement strengthens risk control while reducing unnecessary manual effort.
- Automation is most valuable when it removes repetitive control work without removing review judgment.

31. Q: How would you prepare for an external audit of a restricted project?
A: Show answer
Audit readiness should begin before the auditor asks for documents. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires support for special audits of restricted projects, including auditor selection, provision of supporting documents and responses to queries, review of the preliminary audit report, and review of partner audit reports against project requirements. A request tracker should record each auditor request, responsible person, evidence supplied, date provided, and response status. Power Query can assemble recurring project schedules quickly from standardized source files, while VBA can generate audit-request trackers. Audit evidence should be complete, organized, and internally consistent rather than assembled reactively from scattered folders.

32. Q: Why should a Finance Manager review a preliminary audit report before it becomes final?
A: Show answer
CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires review of the preliminary audit report received from the auditor. The purpose is not to remove unfavorable findings simply because they are uncomfortable, but to verify factual accuracy, evidence, interpretation, and management responses. The Finance Manager should compare findings to accounting records and underlying documentation, identify whether the issue is isolated or systemic, and propose a realistic corrective action with an accountable owner and due date. Where management disagrees with a point, the response should be evidence-based and formally documented. Power Query can help identify the population affected by a finding when transaction analysis is required, while VBA can track corrective actions and due dates. The final report and management response should remain part of the audit file so that unresolved issues can be followed through later reviews.

Send to the team:
- "Please validate every draft audit finding against the underlying records and document the agreed corrective action, owner, and due date."
Key point:
- Draft audit review is a fact-checking and corrective-action process.
- Findings should lead to measurable remediation rather than only a written response.

33. Q: How should a Finance Manager track audit corrective actions after the audit closes?
A: Show answer
Corrective actions should remain visible until the underlying problem is actually resolved and the evidence of completion is retained. GWAHS specifically requires implementation of corrective actions arising from internal and external audits. The Finance Manager should maintain a register with finding, root cause, agreed action, owner, deadline, current status, evidence, and management review date. A simple status of "completed" should not be accepted without supporting evidence. Where the finding relates to a recurring process, the corrective action should change the process or control rather than simply clear one transaction. Power Query can consolidate audit findings from different periods and identify overdue actions, while VBA can produce management follow-up reports. Periodic review should also assess whether the implemented control is operating effectively rather than assuming that implementation alone proves success.

Send to the team:
- "Please keep every audit action open until both the remediation and the supporting evidence have been reviewed."
Key point:
- Audit follow-up should address root causes, not just individual exceptions.
- Evidence of completed remediation is part of the control record.

34. Q: Why should finance systems and spreadsheets have the same control logic?
A: Show answer

Where Excel is used as a reporting layer, the workbook should have controlled inputs, protected formulas, documented mappings, and reconciliation back to the source system. Power Query can import system exports reproducibly, while VBA can prepare controlled outputs without altering source data. The aim is to make the spreadsheet an analytical layer rather than a second uncontrolled accounting system.

Send to the team:

A Finance Manager should therefore view team design as part of control design. Critical processes need named owners, backup arrangements, review points, escalation rules, and adequate segregation between preparation and approval. Workload should be monitored so that important reconciliations and grant reports do not depend on one individual. Training should focus on the actual finance process, including coding, reconciliations, grant rules, systems, and documentation standards. Power Query and VBA can reduce repetitive administrative work, allowing staff capacity to be directed toward exception review and analysis. A useful manager should also review recurring errors by process and use coaching to address root causes rather than simply correcting each transaction individually.

41. Q: Why should finance reporting distinguish slow project spending from a cash shortfall?
A: Show answer
Slow spending can result in funds remaining unused, while a cash shortfall can arise even when a project has adequate approved budget. These are different management issues. CIFOR-ICRAF specifically requires review of donor receivables and payables and reminders to project leaders where spending is slow, while also requiring donor receivables to be collected promptly. The Finance Manager should therefore report approved funding, actual expenditure, expected future spending, donor amounts due, and cash availability separately. A project may be underspending because implementation is delayed, or it may have strong spending but inadequate cash because a donor reimbursement is late. Power Query can combine budget, actual, receivable, and receipt data into a cash-and-utilization view, while VBA can generate alerts for slow spending and overdue receipts. This distinction helps management address the real problem instead of treating every variance as a liquidity issue.

Send to the team:
- "Please report budget utilization, projected spending, donor receivables, and available cash separately when assessing project financial status."
Key point:
- Budget utilization and liquidity are related but not interchangeable.
- Separating the two improves management action and cash planning.

A: Show answer
The objective of partner monitoring is to obtain sufficient assurance that funds are being used in accordance with the agreement while keeping responsibilities clear. CIFOR-ICRAF requires monitoring of restricted-grant partners, analysis of partner expenditure trends, review of partner financial reports, and confirmation that partners spend within budget. The Finance Manager should define the partner reporting timetable, required financial information, review tests, escalation thresholds, and follow-up process. Monitoring should compare expenditure to budget by line, investigate significant variances, verify reported balances, and follow up on unused funds. Power Query can transform multiple partner templates into a common structure, while VBA can produce a partner-monitoring checklist and aging summary. The review should identify issues that require corrective action, additional clarification, or escalation under the partnership agreement rather than simply accumulating observations without resolution.

Send to the team:
- "Please monitor partner expenditure against the approved budget and document every material variance and required follow-up."
Key point:
- Partner monitoring is an assurance process, not a replacement for partner management.

44. Q: How should a Finance Manager review project expenditure for eligibility when multiple cost categories are involved?
A: Show answer
Restricted projects can include partnerships, travel, supplies, consultants, publications, staff costs, and other categories, and CIFOR-ICRAF specifically identifies these areas in annual and rolling budget collaboration. The review should not simply ask whether the total project is below budget. Each material transaction should be considered against the relevant budget line, grant condition, project purpose, timing, supporting evidence, and authorization. Finance should also assess whether the transaction is charged to the correct project rather than a nearby project with available funding. Power Query can map transaction categories to approved budget lines and identify unusual or unclassified records. VBA can generate a pre-reporting eligibility exception schedule. When the donor agreement is ambiguous, the matter should be escalated and resolved before the cost is included in the financial report. This approach protects the integrity of both the ledger and the donor claim.

Send to the team:
- "Please check project, budget line, timing, approval, evidence, and donor eligibility for material restricted-grant expenditure before reporting."
Key point:
- Eligibility is a multi-factor review, not only a budget-availability test.

48. Q: How should a Finance Manager use exception-based review across a high-volume finance operation?
A: Show answer
A senior finance function should not spend equal manual effort on every transaction when many transactions follow established rules. The Finance Manager should define exception criteria such as unmatched bank items, missing approvals, unusual amounts, unsupported card transactions, incorrect project codes, overdue receivables, grant budget overruns, missing timesheets, partner balances, or late donor reports. Routine items can move through standardized processing while exceptions receive focused review. This approach fits both GWAHS's operational finance environment and CIFOR-ICRAF's recurring grant-control requirements. Power Query is well suited to creating normalized exception datasets from multiple source files, while VBA can format the review list, assign owners, and track resolution. Exception criteria should be tested periodically so the process does not miss meaningful risks or produce an unmanageable number of false positives. The Finance Manager remains responsible for deciding whether a flagged item is acceptable, corrected, or escalated.

Send to the team:
- "Please automate routine matching and direct manual review toward defined exceptions and unresolved control breaches."
Key point:
- Exception-based review makes senior finance capacity more effective.
- Exception rules should be reviewed so they remain relevant to actual financial risks.

Q&A NGO Accounting
1. Q: How would you establish effective financial management across an NGO country programme with multiple projects, donors, and operational teams?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should begin with a clear financial-management framework that connects accounting, budgeting, forecasting, cash management, grants, procurement, payroll, reporting, internal controls, and audit requirements. The objective is not merely to ensure transactions are posted correctly; it is to make sure financial information is complete, timely, properly classified, supported by evidence, and useful for operational decisions. A strong framework normally defines the chart of accounts, project and donor dimensions, delegated authorities, review responsibilities, reporting calendars, bank controls, reconciliations, and escalation thresholds. The Finance Manager should also identify which controls are preventative and which are detective, then monitor whether they actually operate. In a field environment, the finance function must balance headquarters requirements with practical operational realities, including staff advances, partner expenditure, cash transfers, multiple currencies, and changing programme priorities. Power Query can consolidate accounting extracts, budget files, donor schedules, and operational reports into one review model, while VBA can generate recurring control checklists and exception reports. The Finance Manager should retain evidence of review rather than merely producing the report, because auditability depends on demonstrating what was checked, when it was checked, and what action followed.

Send to the team:
- "Please manage finance as one integrated control system linking accounting, budgeting, cash, grants, reporting, compliance, and audit evidence."
Key point:
- Financial management is broader than transaction processing and should support both control and programme decision-making.
- A documented control framework should connect systems, responsibilities, reconciliations, reporting, and follow-up.
Official links:
- Head of Finance — DevelopmentAid
- Impactpool vacancy — 1233919
2. Q: Why should month-end closing be managed through a formal timetable rather than treated as a final accounting exercise?
A: Show answer
Month-end closing is a control process that brings together cut-off, accruals, prepayments, advances, payroll, bank reconciliation, receivables, payables, donor balances, interoffice transactions, and management review. A Finance Manager should establish deadlines for each input, identify the responsible person, define the evidence required, and specify who performs the final review. A late invoice should not simply be posted in the following month without considering the underlying service period. Likewise, an unreconciled balance should be investigated rather than carried forward indefinitely. The close pack should show significant movements from the previous month and identify unresolved exceptions. In a donor-funded environment, month-end accuracy affects project expenditure reporting, budget monitoring, cash requests, forecasts, and potentially donor claims. Power Query can consolidate ledger extracts, bank data, payroll data, and supporting registers into a single close model. VBA can prepare the close checklist, flag incomplete schedules, and create an exception summary for management. The Finance Manager should sign off key reconciliations and maintain the review evidence so the close is reproducible even when team members change.

Send to the team:
- "Please follow the month-end timetable and do not treat unresolved reconciliations as completed work simply because the reporting deadline has arrived."
Key point:
- Month-end close should be controlled through deadlines, ownership, reconciliations, and evidence.
- A good close process identifies and escalates exceptions before reports are finalized.
Official links:
- Head of Finance — DevelopmentAid
- Impactpool vacancy — 1233919
3. Q: How would you manage year-end closing when accounting, donor reporting, audit preparation, and management reporting all compete for attention?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should separate the year-end process into controlled workstreams rather than allowing audit preparation to become an emergency after the ledger has already been closed. The workstreams should normally include transaction cut-off, balance-sheet reconciliations, accruals, prepayments, payroll liabilities, fixed assets, cash and bank balances, advances, receivables, payables, donor and grant balances, interoffice transactions, tax liabilities, and financial statement preparation. A year-end schedule should identify the evidence required for each balance and the person responsible for obtaining it. Donor closeout should also be considered where projects have ended because unspent balances, questioned costs, outstanding claims, or final reports may need separate reconciliation. The Finance Manager should compare the final trial balance to the previous year, investigate large movements, and ensure that audit support agrees to the ledger rather than creating a separate unofficial data set. Power Query can combine closing schedules and identify differences between subledgers and the general ledger, while VBA can produce an audit-request tracker with ownership and status. Senior review should focus on material risks and unusual movements rather than checking every transaction equally.

Send to the team:
- "Please complete year-end through a documented close plan that connects the ledger, reconciliations, donor schedules, financial statements, and audit evidence."
Key point:
- Year-end requires coordinated accounting, reporting, donor, and audit controls.
- The final ledger and supporting schedules must tell the same financial story.

4. Q: How would you review balance-sheet reconciliations as a Finance Manager rather than simply checking whether the reconciliation has been prepared?
A: Show answer
A management-level review should test whether the balance has a clear composition, whether the supporting schedule agrees with the ledger, whether reconciling items are legitimate, and whether old items have been investigated. Bank accounts, advances, receivables, payables, taxes, accruals, prepayments, payroll liabilities, fixed assets, and intercompany balances should have defined supporting schedules. A reconciliation showing only "difference = zero" is not sufficient if the underlying items are unexplained. The Finance Manager should review ageing, investigate unusual or stale balances, confirm whether balances belong to the correct project or donor, and ensure that adjustments are supported by documentation and proper approval. For partner advances, the schedule should connect transfers, financial reports, eligible expenditure, deductions, returns, and remaining balances. Power Query can compare the general ledger with subledgers and identify old items, missing references, or unexplained differences. VBA can produce a standardized reconciliation cover sheet with preparer, reviewer, date, balance, exceptions, and action status. This shifts reconciliation from a clerical exercise to a management control.

Send to the team:
- "Please make every balance-sheet reconciliation explain the balance, list reconciling items, and show evidence of review and follow-up."
Key point:
- A reconciliation is a control explanation, not simply a zero-difference calculation.
- Ageing and unresolved items should be part of Finance Manager review.

5. Q: How would you build a useful cash-flow projection for an NGO with donor receipts, programme expenditure, partner transfers, and uncertain timing?
A: Show answer
Cash-flow forecasting should distinguish committed cash requirements from uncertain or discretionary items. The Finance Manager should begin with opening bank balances, expected donor receipts, payroll, statutory payments, supplier commitments, partner advances, programme activities, taxes, rent, travel, and other known obligations. The forecast should be linked to the funding schedule and project spending plan rather than relying only on historical averages. It should also consider restricted cash and any restrictions on how funds may be used. A weekly or rolling forecast can be particularly useful where programmes operate in volatile environments because timing can change quickly. Variances between forecast and actual cash movements should be analysed and used to improve future assumptions. Power Query can combine bank statements, payment pipelines, grant schedules, payroll, and budget data, while VBA can refresh the forecast, calculate projected closing cash, and flag dates where liquidity falls below a management threshold. The Finance Manager should present scenarios where necessary rather than giving management one apparently precise number that hides uncertainty.

Send to the team:
- "Please maintain a rolling cash forecast that reconciles opening cash, expected receipts, committed payments, planned activities, and projected closing balances."
Key point:
- Cash-flow forecasting should be linked to actual obligations and funding timing.
- Scenario analysis is more useful than false precision when donor receipts or programme spending are uncertain.

6. Q: How would you control cash transfers to field offices or projects so that operational needs are met without weakening financial accountability?
A: Show answer
Cash transfers should be based on approved budgets, realistic cash requirements, previous utilization, outstanding advances, and documented authorization. A Finance Manager should avoid sending new funds simply because a field office requests them; the request should be assessed against the existing balance and expected expenditure. The transfer schedule should identify project, donor, cost centre, currency, amount, date, receiving account, approval, and subsequent liquidation or reporting. Unused funds should be monitored and returned or reallocated in accordance with policy. Where transfers are internal, accounting should distinguish the movement of cash from external income and avoid presenting internal funding as donor revenue. A cross-office reconciliation should confirm that the sending and receiving records agree. Power Query can compare approved transfer requests, bank movements, and field-office acknowledgements. VBA can generate a transfer register, identify overdue liquidations, and prepare a list of balances requiring follow-up. The Finance Manager should monitor concentration risk and ensure that cash is not transferred without adequate supporting information.

Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile every field-office cash transfer to an approved request and follow up immediately on unretired or unsupported balances."
Key point:
- Cash transfer authorization and subsequent liquidation are part of the same control cycle.
- Internal transfers should remain distinguishable from external revenue and donor income.

7. Q: How would you strengthen bank reconciliation where the programme generates a high volume of transactions across multiple accounts or currencies?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should define a standard reconciliation method for each bank account, with the bank statement treated as the external control record and the ledger or cashbook as the internal record. The process should identify timing differences, bank charges, direct debits, unidentified receipts, transfers in transit, duplicate entries, and transactions not yet posted. In foreign-currency accounts, the reconciliation must also distinguish transaction-level matching from period-end retranslation. Old unmatched items should be escalated rather than repeatedly rolled forward. Power Query can import multiple bank statement formats, standardize dates and references, normalize currencies, and match transactions against the cashbook using amount, date, reference, and tolerance rules. VBA can generate the reconciliation summary, categorize exceptions, and prepare a review sheet. The Finance Manager should ensure that no automated matching rule is allowed to clear an item without an appropriate level of confidence and that unusual matches are subject to human review.

Send to the team:
- "Please use standardized bank-reconciliation rules and investigate aged or unusual unmatched transactions before close."
Key point:
- Automated matching should accelerate routine items while preserving human review for exceptions.
- Multi-currency bank controls require both reconciliation and disciplined FX treatment.

8. Q: Why should petty cash controls include surprise cash counts rather than relying only on the monthly cashbook?
A: Show answer
Petty cash is vulnerable because physical cash can move outside the normal bank-control environment. A Finance Manager should establish a fixed float or documented authorization mechanism, require receipts and approvals, define custody responsibilities, and perform regular reconciliations between physical cash, vouchers, and the ledger. Surprise cash counts are useful because they provide an independent test of whether the book balance matches the physical balance at an unannounced time. Differences should be documented immediately and escalated according to policy rather than simply corrected through an unexplained adjustment. The review should also look for unusually large cash withdrawals, repeated claims, missing receipts, split transactions, and transactions outside approved categories. Power Query can consolidate petty-cash logs from multiple locations and identify unusually frequent or high-value transactions. VBA can generate cash-count forms and exception summaries. The control objective is not merely to balance the cashbook; it is to make misuse, weak documentation, and leakage more difficult to conceal.

Send to the team:
- "Please perform periodic documented cash counts and investigate every difference between physical cash, vouchers, and the accounting record."
Key point:
- Physical verification is an important complement to ledger reconciliation.
- Cash exceptions should be investigated rather than silently adjusted.

9. Q: How would you control staff advances so that field teams can operate while outstanding balances remain visible and recoverable?
A: Show answer
Staff advances should be managed as temporary receivables with a clear purpose, authorization, issue date, expected settlement date, and responsible employee. The Finance Manager should monitor whether advances are settled within policy, verify the supporting documents, compare the actual expenditure with the advance, and either recover unused funds or reimburse a legitimate shortfall. New advances should be restricted where existing advances remain overdue unless an authorized exception exists. An ageing report should classify items by days outstanding so management can focus on the oldest balances. The register should agree to the general ledger and include project and donor dimensions where relevant. Power Query can consolidate advance registers with payment records and expense claims, while VBA can calculate ageing, produce reminders, and prepare exception lists. The Finance Manager should pay particular attention to large balances, repeated overdue settlements, staff with multiple open advances, and balances remaining at reporting dates, because these issues can distort project expenditure and receivable positions.

Send to the team:
- "Please keep every staff advance open until it is fully supported, cleared, or formally approved for recovery or exception handling."
Key point:
- Staff advances are controlled receivables, not permanent expense accounts.
- Ageing and exception-based follow-up are essential for preventing old balances.

10. Q: How would you assess and monitor partner advances or subawards throughout their full financial lifecycle?
A: Show answer
Partner and subaward controls should begin before the first transfer and continue through closeout. The Finance Manager should review partner financial capacity, budget, reporting arrangements, bank information, authorization requirements, record-retention provisions, and the terms governing eligible expenditure. Once funds are transferred, the partner balance should be tracked through opening advance, subsequent transfers, reported expenditure, questioned or unsupported costs, returns, and closing balance. A partner's expenditure report should not automatically be treated as eligible simply because it has been submitted; the organization should perform appropriate financial review and reconcile the report to the agreement, approved budget, and supporting evidence. Monitoring should be proportionate to risk and can include desk reviews, transaction sampling, field visits, and capacity-building actions. Power Query can standardize different partner reporting templates into one monitoring model, while VBA can create ageing reports, overdue-report alerts, and partner action logs. The Finance Manager should ensure that identified weaknesses result in documented corrective actions rather than merely repeated comments in review notes.

Send to the team:
- "Please monitor every partner advance from transfer through financial reporting, reconciliation, corrective action, and closeout."
Key point:
- Partner finance is both an accounting control and a programme assurance process.

11. Q: Why is correct project, grant, fund, and account coding a Finance Manager responsibility rather than only a data-entry issue?
A: Show answer
Coding determines where expenditure, income, assets, and liabilities appear in management and donor reporting. A transaction can be mathematically correct and still produce an inaccurate report if it is posted to the wrong project, donor, fund, cost centre, or account. The Finance Manager should ensure that the chart of accounts and dimensional structure support operational and donor reporting needs without creating unnecessary complexity. Coding guidance should explain ambiguous cases and define how corrections are approved. Finance should also compare actual transactions with approved budgets and donor restrictions so misclassifications can be identified promptly. Power Query can map source-system codes to a controlled master list and flag unrecognized or inactive combinations. VBA can stop or flag incomplete journal batches before posting and produce a coding-exception report for review. This becomes especially important where headquarters-posted transactions, shared costs, or cross-charges are involved, because poor classification can move costs between grants and distort funding performance.

Send to the team:
- "Please validate project, grant, fund, cost-centre, and account coding before posting and escalate unclear classifications instead of guessing."
Key point:
- Coding controls protect both financial statements and donor/project reporting.
- Master-data validation should occur before incorrect classifications reach the ledger.

12. Q: How would you control ERP or SAP financial data when transactions can originate from different offices and systems?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should understand how source transactions enter the ERP, which fields are mandatory, which users have posting rights, and how headquarters or shared-service transactions flow into the country records. Access should follow role-based responsibilities, with segregation between preparation, approval, posting, and review where feasible. Interface or upload processes need validation so that totals, account codes, project dimensions, currencies, and posting dates agree with the source. Periodic reports should be reconciled to source schedules, particularly when manual journals or headquarters-posted entries are used. Where corrections are necessary, the original transaction should remain traceable through an authorized adjustment. Power Query can extract ERP data for independent analytical review without altering the source ledger, while VBA can generate standardized reconciliation and exception packs. The Finance Manager should be cautious about spreadsheets becoming an unofficial substitute for the ERP; spreadsheets should support analysis and control, not create a parallel ledger that cannot be reconciled to the accounting system.

Send to the team:
- "Please treat the ERP as the controlled accounting record and use spreadsheets for analysis, reconciliation, and exception review only."
Key point:
- System access, interface controls, and source-to-ledger reconciliation are central to ERP reliability.
- Spreadsheet automation should strengthen the system of record rather than create a second uncontrolled ledger.

13. Q: How would you review headquarters-posted transactions and interoffice balances?
A: Show answer
Transactions posted centrally can be efficient, but they create a reconciliation risk if the country office cannot easily trace the posting to its underlying support. The Finance Manager should maintain visibility over transaction reference, project, donor, account, amount, currency, posting date, and originating office. Interoffice or intercompany balances should reconcile in both locations and should be cleared through agreed procedures rather than remaining unresolved at period-end. Cross-charges should have a clear basis, such as documented allocation methodology, and should not move restricted costs into a project simply to solve a budget problem. The Finance Manager should also distinguish internal funding transfers from external donor income. Power Query can compare headquarters transaction extracts with country-ledger records and flag unmatched or differently coded items. VBA can build a monthly interoffice reconciliation pack and ageing report. Senior review should concentrate on old balances, unusual allocations, and entries posted near reporting deadlines because these can have a disproportionate impact on project and country reporting.

Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile headquarters and country-office postings each period and document the basis for every material cross-charge or allocation."
Key point:
- Internal transactions require two-sided reconciliation and clear allocation logic.
- Central posting should never eliminate country-level accountability for coding and evidence.

14. Q: How would you ensure donor financial reports remain compliant with grant terms rather than simply agreeing to the general ledger?
A: Show answer
Donor reporting requires more than arithmetic agreement to the ledger. The Finance Manager should review the grant agreement, approved budget, reporting period, eligible cost categories, cost-share or co-financing requirements where applicable, exchange-rate rules, supporting-document standards, and submission deadlines. The accounting system should contain sufficient dimensions to extract grant-specific information, while a separate donor reconciliation should connect the ledger to the report submitted. Restricted or specifically conditioned funding should be monitored so that expenditure is not recognized or charged outside the permitted scope. Variances should be explained consistently with programme activity and management forecasts. Power Query can map ledger transactions to donor reporting categories, compare submitted reports with current-period data, and identify differences. VBA can generate report checklists, populate recurring schedules, and track submission status. The Finance Manager should retain the submitted report, final supporting schedules, approval evidence, and subsequent correction history so an auditor or donor can reconstruct the process.
