15. Q: Why should restricted contract balances and restricted-income debtors be monitored separately from ordinary receivables?
A: Show answer
A donor-related balance may have conditions that affect recognition, recoverability, expenditure eligibility, or timing of reimbursement. The Finance Manager should therefore understand whether a balance represents an approved reimbursement claim, an advance, deferred or restricted funding, or another contractual position. The supporting schedule should show the grant, funding period, claim status, amount, expenditure basis, expected recovery or release, and responsible person. This allows management to distinguish a genuine receivable from an amount that is still subject to donor review or additional conditions. Ageing should be monitored because old donor balances can affect liquidity and may indicate disputes or incomplete submissions. 16. Q: How would you manage restricted funding so that programme teams understand both what money is available and what conditions apply?
A: Show answer
Restricted funding should be tracked at the level needed to distinguish the donor's conditions from the organization's general operating resources. The Finance Manager should ensure that grant budgets, reporting categories, funding periods, and restrictions are reflected in the accounting structure and management schedules. Cash availability should not be confused with unrestricted availability; a large bank balance does not automatically mean that all funds can be redirected to another purpose. Monthly review should compare opening restricted balances, receipts, eligible expenditure, releases or recognized income where applicable, and closing balances. Power Query can connect donor schedules with actual expenditure and cash receipts, while VBA can generate monthly restricted-funding roll-forwards and flag transactions posted outside approved categories. Management should review projected utilization because both over-spending and under-spending can create grant-management problems. The Finance Manager should ensure that restrictions are communicated to programme and budget holders in operational language rather than assuming accounting codes alone will prevent misuse.

17. Q: How would you control payables, accruals, and liabilities so that expense recognition is not driven only by invoice receipt?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should establish a cut-off process that considers when goods or services were received, whether the obligation exists at period-end, and whether the amount can be measured reliably under the organization's accounting framework. Supplier invoices arriving after month-end should be assessed for the service period rather than automatically pushed into the following month. Accrual schedules should include vendor, service description, project, donor, estimated amount, source of estimate, date recorded, and subsequent reversal or settlement. Payables should be aged and reviewed for unusual old balances, duplicate invoices, disputed items, and invoices with missing approvals. Power Query can compare procurement records, receiving documentation, supplier invoices, and subsequent payments to identify likely accruals and duplicate obligations. VBA can produce an accrual register, reversal schedule, and month-end checklist. The Finance Manager should ensure that estimates are supported and updated as better information becomes available, since stale accruals can distort project expenditure and the liability position.

18. Q: How would you control prepayments so that programme expenditure is not distorted by large upfront payments?
A: Show answer
Prepayments should be identified when cash is paid before the corresponding benefit or service period. The Finance Manager should maintain a register showing supplier, purpose, project, donor, amount, start date, end date, allocation basis, monthly release, and remaining balance. The schedule should agree to the general ledger and should be reviewed for expired or abandoned items. For rent, insurance, subscriptions, or similar services, the release should follow the period of benefit unless another policy basis applies. Where a prepayment relates to a grant with a fixed implementation period, the Finance Manager should ensure that release timing does not create expenditure outside the eligible period. Power Query can combine supplier payments and contract periods to identify likely prepayments, while VBA can calculate scheduled releases and produce journals for review. Management should also assess recoverability where a supplier cannot deliver the remaining service. This helps protect both balance-sheet accuracy and donor budget reporting.

Send to the team:

Key point:
- Upfront cash payment does not necessarily mean immediate expense recognition.

19. Q: How would you distinguish budget monitoring from genuine financial forecasting?
A: Show answer
Budget monitoring compares actual and committed expenditure with the approved plan, while forecasting asks what is likely to happen next based on current information. A Finance Manager should not simply annualize current expenditure and call it a forecast. Forecasting should consider implementation progress, procurement pipelines, staffing plans, partner spending, known commitments, funding constraints, and changes in programme activity. The review should distinguish timing variances from structural variances and should identify whether an underspend is temporary or likely to persist. Budget-holder discussions should focus on assumptions and corrective action rather than only on historical percentages. Power Query can combine actuals, commitments, procurement pipelines, payroll, partner forecasts, and grant budgets. VBA can update scenario tables and produce variance dashboards. Senior management should receive explanations for material movements and clear implications for cash, donor commitments, and programme delivery.

Send to the team:
- "Please explain every material forecast movement through operational assumptions rather than simply extrapolating the latest actual expenditure."
Key point:
- Budget monitoring describes performance against plan; forecasting estimates the future.
- A credible forecast combines accounting data with operational knowledge.

20. Q: How would you support annual budgeting while keeping programme ownership with non-finance managers?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should provide the framework, templates, assumptions, deadlines, and challenge process, while programme managers remain accountable for the operational decisions behind their budgets. A useful budget process starts with activities and resource requirements, then translates them into cost categories, timing, staffing, procurement, partner transfers, and shared-cost assumptions. Finance should test the budget for arithmetic consistency, funding restrictions, allocation logic, affordability, and alignment with strategic priorities. Once approved, the budget should be loaded into a controlled model so actuals and commitments can be compared consistently. Power Query can combine budget submissions from different teams into a standardized structure, while VBA can validate required fields, calculate totals, and produce consolidation packs. Finance should also document assumptions because the quality of a future forecast depends on knowing why the original budget was constructed. The Finance Manager's role is therefore both technical control and constructive challenge.

Send to the team:
- "Please submit budgets using the standard activity, project, funding, timing, and cost-category structure and document the assumptions behind significant estimates."
Key point:
- Finance owns the budgeting process and controls, while programme managers own operational assumptions.

21. Q: How would you investigate a significant budget variance that is caused by both under-spending and delayed programme implementation?
A: Show answer
The first step is to determine whether the variance is a timing issue, a genuine reduction in expected activity, a coding error, a procurement delay, or a combination of causes. The Finance Manager should compare actual expenditure, commitments, procurement status, staffing, partner implementation, and the current programme workplan. An underspend is not automatically positive; it can indicate that planned outputs have not been delivered or that donor resources may remain unused. The analysis should therefore connect financial variance to operational performance. Where expenditure is delayed, the forecast should be updated and any donor implications assessed. Power Query can compare budget, actuals, commitments, purchase orders, and implementation data where available, while VBA can create a variance-exception report by project or budget line. The Finance Manager should distinguish unavoidable timing differences from issues requiring management intervention and document the resulting action plan.

Send to the team:
- "Please explain material variances through the underlying operational cause and update the forecast and action plan accordingly."
Key point:
- A financial variance should be interpreted in operational context.

22. Q: How would you design delegated authority and segregation-of-duties controls that still work in a small field office?
A: Show answer
Segregation of duties should be designed around the risks in the transaction cycle rather than assuming a large number of staff exist. The Finance Manager should separate initiation, approval, processing, payment authorization, recording, and reconciliation as far as practical. Where staffing limitations prevent full separation, compensating controls should be documented, such as independent review of bank statements, payment registers, supporting documents, or system reports by a senior person. Delegated authorities should state financial thresholds, permitted approvers, and any restrictions on self-approval or related-party transactions. Bank mandates and account signatories should also be reviewed when staff change. Power Query can support independent analysis of payment data to identify unusual self-approval patterns, repeated vendors, or transactions above defined thresholds. VBA can create payment-control checklists and route exceptions for review. The Finance Manager should periodically test whether the control design is actually operating rather than assuming that the written matrix guarantees compliance.

Send to the team:
- "Please apply delegated authority and segregation-of-duties rules to every payment cycle and document compensating controls where staffing limits prevent full separation."
Key point:
- Small offices need risk-based controls, not abandoned controls.
- Compensating review must be explicit when complete segregation is impractical.

23. Q: How would you approach fraud-risk management without treating every unusual transaction as evidence of fraud?
A: Show answer
Fraud-risk management starts with understanding where opportunity, pressure, weak oversight, or override risk can arise. The Finance Manager should identify vulnerable points such as cash handling, supplier creation, payment approvals, payroll changes, advances, procurement, partner transfers, and manual journals. Controls should then be designed to prevent unauthorized activity and detect unusual patterns. An exception does not prove misconduct; it is a signal requiring documented review. Investigations should follow organizational procedures, preserve evidence, protect confidentiality, and escalate through the appropriate governance channels. Finance should not conduct informal accusations based solely on spreadsheet anomalies. Power Query can identify duplicate bank accounts, repeated invoice numbers, unusual payment timing, vendor concentration, or transactions outside normal ranges. VBA can compile exception cases into a controlled review log. Senior Finance leadership should also monitor recurring control failures because a weakness that permits one irregular transaction may create a wider exposure.

Send to the team:
- "Please treat unusual transactions as documented exceptions requiring review and escalation under the organization's established fraud procedures."
Key point:
- Fraud controls should focus on prevention, detection, evidence, and proper escalation.
- An automated anomaly flag is a control signal, not a conclusion about misconduct.

24. Q: How would you protect the organization against waste and financial leakage in programme implementation?
A: Show answer
Waste and leakage can arise through poor forecasting, duplicate purchases, unused advances, inactive commitments, weak vendor controls, avoidable bank charges, expired contracts, or expenditure that does not achieve the intended programme purpose. The Finance Manager should use management reporting to identify unusual spending patterns and then discuss the operational cause with budget holders. Control should not mean indiscriminate cost reduction; a low-cost option can be inappropriate if it does not meet programme requirements, while a higher cost can be justified where it prevents operational failure. The focus should be value, authorization, eligibility, and evidence. Power Query can consolidate procurement, invoice, payment, and budget data to identify repetitive or unusual patterns, while VBA can generate monthly leakage-exception reports. The Finance Manager should ensure that savings or corrective actions are recorded and that management sees whether the issue was resolved. This turns financial analysis into a practical resource-stewardship function rather than a purely accounting exercise.

Send to the team:
- "Please investigate recurring exceptions, unused commitments, duplicate-looking transactions, and avoidable costs and document the corrective action."
Key point:
- Waste prevention combines financial analysis with operational understanding.
- Exception monitoring should lead to corrective action rather than repeated observations.

25. Q: How would you prepare the finance function for an external donor or statutory audit?
A: Show answer
Audit readiness should be continuous rather than beginning when the auditors arrive. The Finance Manager should maintain a schedule of key balances, reconciliations, donor reports, contracts, approvals, bank evidence, fixed-asset records, partner reports, tax filings, payroll records, and prior audit recommendations. Supporting documents should be organized so that a transaction can be traced from source document to approval, posting, report, and payment. The Finance Manager should also understand the applicable donor and statutory requirements and maintain a calendar of filings and submissions. When auditors request information, responses should be coordinated through an audit tracker with owner, deadline, evidence, and status. Power Query can help reconcile audit-request samples to the ledger and identify missing fields before information is submitted. VBA can produce an audit-request register and ageing report. The objective is not merely to answer questions quickly, but to demonstrate that the underlying control environment is functioning consistently throughout the year.

Send to the team:
- "Please maintain audit evidence throughout the year and update the audit-request tracker as soon as a request is received."

26. Q: How should a Finance Manager respond to audit findings so that recommendations become real improvements rather than repeated observations?
A: Show answer
Each audit recommendation should be converted into a documented action with a clear root cause, responsible owner, due date, evidence requirement, and status. The Finance Manager should distinguish between immediate correction of the specific finding and the broader control improvement needed to prevent recurrence. For example, correcting one unsupported transaction is not sufficient if the underlying issue is weak approval or filing procedures. Management should receive periodic reports on overdue recommendations, risk severity, and barriers to implementation. Where management accepts residual risk, the decision should be documented through the appropriate governance route. Power Query can aggregate audit recommendations from different reports and categorize them by owner, risk, and status. VBA can generate ageing dashboards and escalation packs for management review. Closure should require evidence that the agreed control is operating, not simply an email stating that the action was completed.

Send to the team:
- "Please close audit recommendations only when the corrective action is implemented and supporting evidence has been reviewed."
Key point:
- Audit follow-up should address root causes, not only individual errors.
- Recommendation closure requires evidence of implementation and, where appropriate, operating effectiveness.

27. Q: How would you ensure statutory tax and government remittances are completed accurately and on time?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should maintain a statutory calendar covering tax deductions, payroll-related remittances, withholding obligations, registrations, reporting deadlines, and other applicable government requirements. Each liability should reconcile to the ledger and the supporting calculation, with evidence of the filing and payment retained. The finance team should not treat a payment confirmation as sufficient evidence if the underlying return or calculation has not been completed. Changes in local law or donor requirements should be communicated to the appropriate teams and reflected in procedures. Where taxes are withheld from supplier payments, the Finance Manager should make sure the vendor invoice, withholding calculation, payment amount, and tax liability all reconcile. Power Query can consolidate payroll and supplier tax data to identify missing or inconsistent withholding amounts, while VBA can generate a statutory deadline tracker. Senior review should concentrate on missed deadlines, unusual tax balances, unreconciled liabilities, and recurring adjustments.

Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile every statutory liability to its underlying calculation and retain evidence of both filing and payment."
Key point:
- Tax compliance requires calculation, reconciliation, filing, payment, and evidence.
- A statutory calendar helps prevent late obligations from being discovered during close or audit.

28. Q: How would you use financial systems and data-quality controls to reduce reporting errors before they reach senior management?
A: Show answer
Data quality should be controlled at several stages: source input, master-data validation, transaction processing, interface or upload, reconciliation, and final reporting. The Finance Manager should identify critical fields such as project, donor, account, currency, date, vendor, funding source, and reference number, then define validation rules for each. Duplicate records, invalid codes, missing dimensions, unexpected currencies, and unusual values should be surfaced before reporting. Changes to master data should be controlled so that inactive projects or accounts cannot continue to receive transactions without an approved exception. Power Query is useful for standardizing source files, checking data types, comparing tables, identifying duplicates, and creating exception lists. VBA can produce pre-close validation reports and prevent final output generation when mandatory control checks are incomplete. The Finance Manager should resist the temptation to resolve every data-quality problem manually because repeated manual correction hides structural weaknesses that need process redesign.

Send to the team:
- "Please correct recurring data-quality problems at the source and use exception reporting to identify records that fail validation."
Key point:
- Good reporting depends on controlled master data and repeatable validation rules.
- Automation should expose recurring data problems so processes can be improved at source.

Management reporting should do more than reproduce the trial balance. A good report distinguishes facts from assumptions and highlights what action is required. Programme managers should see enough detail to manage their budgets, while senior leadership should receive a concise view of material risks and strategic implications. Power Query can create a repeatable data model that feeds project, donor, cost-centre, and consolidated dashboards without repeated copy-paste. VBA can refresh standard management packs and populate recurring narrative or exception sections. The Finance Manager should also review whether users act on the information; a technically accurate report that nobody understands is not an effective management control.

Review meetings should focus on what happened, why it happened, what is likely to happen next, and what action is required. Finance should also document escalation thresholds so that managers know when a variance requires Finance Manager or senior-management attention. 31. Q: How would you manage finance-team performance while maintaining strong control discipline during periods of heavy workload?
A: Show answer

Work should be prioritized according to financial risk and reporting impact rather than simply according to who asks first. Recurring tasks such as reconciliations, payroll schedules, partner reports, donor reporting, and cash requests should have documented procedures and backup coverage. Coaching should focus on errors' underlying causes, not only correction of individual mistakes. The Finance Manager should monitor workload so that excessive pressure does not cause critical reviews to be skipped. VBA can create team task trackers and highlight overdue control activities, while Power Query can consolidate status information from recurring schedules. Training should also cover the rationale behind key controls because staff are more likely to apply a procedure consistently when they understand the risk it addresses. Strong supervision means maintaining both delivery and control quality.

Send to the team:

32. Q: How would you build finance capacity among programme staff, partners, and other non-finance colleagues?
A: Show answer

Programme staff may need support with budgeting, forecasting, coding, documentation, and variance analysis; partners may need support with financial reporting, supporting evidence, reconciliations, and subaward requirements. The Finance Manager should use examples from real transactions rather than relying entirely on generic presentations. Training effectiveness should be checked through subsequent reviews and recurring-error analysis. Where partner capacity is weaker, the response may include templates, reporting calendars, coaching, transaction-level review, or increased monitoring rather than a single training session. Power Query can standardize partner submissions and provide feedback on common data problems, while VBA can create reusable reporting templates and validation routines. Training materials should explain both the procedure and the reason for the control, helping staff understand how poor documentation can affect donor compliance, audit results, and project performance.

33. Q: How would you conduct partner financial due diligence before entering or renewing a partnership arrangement?
A: Show answer
Due diligence should assess whether the partner has sufficient financial capacity, governance, documentation, systems, staff capability, bank controls, procurement practices, and experience with donor-funded resources. The Finance Manager should review financial statements or available records, prior audit issues, bank-account controls, segregation of duties, budgeting practices, reporting quality, and any significant weaknesses identified through previous engagements. The assessment should be proportionate to the value and risk of the proposed agreement. Where gaps exist, the agreement can include additional controls, capacity-building actions, reporting requirements, or monitoring arrangements. Due diligence should not be reduced to checking whether the partner has a certificate of registration. Power Query can standardize due-diligence questionnaires and scoring inputs for management review, while VBA can create an evidence checklist and identify incomplete submissions. The Finance Manager should also ensure that any risk-based conditions are communicated to programme teams and included in subsequent monitoring.

34. Q: How would you monitor outsourced accounting services without losing internal ownership of the financial records?
A: Show answer
Outsourcing processing does not transfer accountability for the quality of financial information. The Finance Manager should define the service scope, responsibilities, review points, deadlines, access rights, documentation requirements, reconciliation expectations, and escalation routes. The internal finance team should retain independent oversight of critical balances, bank reconciliations, payment approvals, journal review, and management reporting. Service-provider outputs should be reconciled to source records and checked against agreed service-level standards. Where the provider posts transactions directly into the ERP, user access and audit trails become especially important. Power Query can independently compare provider outputs with internal source schedules and identify missing, duplicate, or unusual entries. VBA can produce service-level checklists and overdue-action reports. The Finance Manager should periodically review whether outsourcing still meets control, continuity, and value requirements and should maintain enough internal knowledge to avoid becoming completely dependent on the provider.

35. Q: How can Finance support business development and resource mobilization without compromising accounting independence?

Once funding is secured, Finance should translate the approved agreement into the accounting structure, reporting calendar, cash plan, and grant monitoring schedule. Finance should not, however, adjust accounting treatment to support an expected fundraising result. Power Query can consolidate historical cost data to inform realistic proposal assumptions, while VBA can build standardized proposal-budget templates and sensitivity analyses. The Finance Manager should also examine whether proposed cost structures create future liabilities, restricted balances, or operational commitments that the organization may not be able to sustain. This makes finance a business partner while preserving financial-control independence.

36. Q: How would you manage financial risk in a humanitarian or field environment where priorities and spending patterns can change rapidly?
A: Show answer
Financial controls in a changing environment should remain strong while allowing management to respond quickly. The Finance Manager should identify critical risks such as cash exposure, supplier availability, emergency procurement, rapid programme expansion, staff advances, partner transfers, foreign-exchange movements, and incomplete documentation caused by operational constraints. Controls should be risk-based and supported by clear delegation and retrospective review where immediate processing is necessary. Cash-flow forecasts should be updated frequently, and field offices should report significant exceptions promptly. Power Query can consolidate field-office financial data rapidly into a common dashboard, while VBA can generate exception lists and management packs when normal monthly reporting cycles are too slow. The Finance Manager should distinguish a genuine emergency control adaptation from an avoidable shortcut and ensure that any temporary exception has an owner, approval, expiry point, and subsequent review. Operational urgency should never become an excuse for undocumented or unreviewed financial activity.

37. Q: How would you manage multi-currency accounting and foreign-exchange exposure across projects and field offices?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should establish consistent rules for transaction recording, period-end retranslation, settlement differences, and presentation currency reporting in accordance with the organization's accounting framework. The same foreign-currency balance can produce different local-currency values at transaction date, reporting date, and settlement, so the method must be documented and repeatable. Donor reporting can add another complication where the grant agreement specifies a particular exchange-rate methodology. The Finance Manager should therefore maintain a clear FX schedule by currency, account, project, and reporting period and reconcile it to the ledger. Power Query can consolidate exchange-rate files and foreign-currency balances for analytical review, while VBA can calculate standardized revaluation schedules and identify unusual FX movements. Management reporting should distinguish operational spending variances from exchange-rate effects so that programme teams do not mistake currency movement for underlying cost performance.

Send to the team:
- "Please apply the approved FX methodology consistently and separate operational variances from exchange-rate effects in management reporting."
Key point:
- Multi-currency control depends on consistent transaction, retranslation, and reporting rules.
- FX movements should be visible separately from genuine programme cost changes.

38. Q: Why should finance policies and procedures be reviewed as operating controls rather than treated as static documents?
A: Show answer
Policies only strengthen control when staff can apply them consistently and when the procedures reflect the organization's actual systems, donor requirements, delegated authorities, and operating environment. The Finance Manager should periodically review recurring exceptions, audit findings, regulatory changes, donor requirements, system changes, and operational changes to identify where procedures need revision. The review should clearly distinguish policy from procedure: policy establishes the control requirement, while procedure explains how staff carry it out. Updates should include approval, effective date, communication, training, and evidence of implementation. Power Query can analyze recurring exception categories across finance processes, while VBA can maintain a policy-review tracker with owners, review dates, and outstanding actions. The Finance Manager should avoid writing excessively complicated procedures that staff cannot follow in practice. A shorter procedure that is consistently applied and monitored may provide stronger control than a lengthy document that no one uses.

Send to the team:
- "Please report recurring control exceptions so Finance can determine whether the underlying policy, procedure, training, or system needs improvement."
Key point:
- Policies should be reviewed against actual operating risks and control performance.
- Procedure updates require communication, training, ownership, and evidence of implementation.

39. Q: How would you establish a strong financial documentation system when records exist in both electronic and paper form across field locations?
A: Show answer
Documentation should be organized around traceability rather than simply storage volume. The Finance Manager should define minimum evidence for each transaction type, file naming conventions, retention requirements, approval evidence, and responsibility for maintaining the official record. Electronic and paper records should be indexed consistently enough that a reviewer can trace the transaction from accounting entry to supporting document. Where original paper documents are required, the organization should maintain the physical record under controlled custody while using electronic copies for working review where appropriate. Power Query can use structured metadata from file names or control registers to identify missing support, while VBA can generate document-completeness checklists tied to transaction references. The Finance Manager should also ensure that finance records remain accessible when staff leave, offices relocate, or programmes close. Poor document management can weaken otherwise sound accounting because an organization may know that a transaction occurred but be unable to prove its authorization, purpose, eligibility, and payment.

Send to the team:

Key point:
- Documentation is part of the control, not an administrative task performed after accounting.
- Records must remain retrievable through staff changes, audits, and donor closeout.

40. Q: How would you manage donor closeout so that final financial reporting, outstanding balances, assets, advances, and audit requirements are all resolved?
A: Show answer
Donor closeout should start before the final reporting deadline. The Finance Manager should review remaining budget, commitments, unliquidated advances, partner balances, receivables, payables, restricted cash, fixed assets, tax liabilities, and expected final claims. The accounting records should be reconciled to the donor's reporting structure and any final corrections should be completed before the final submission where possible. Assets purchased under the grant should be reviewed against donor requirements for retention, transfer, disposal, or continued use. The Finance Manager should also assess whether open partner balances require recovery or additional reporting and whether any expenditure remains subject to donor review. Power Query can compare the final ledger, grant budget, partner schedules, and closeout checklist, while VBA can generate a closeout tracker with outstanding actions and owners. The Finance Manager should preserve the final reporting package and evidence in a way that permits later audit or donor inquiry even after the programme team has moved on to other grants.

Send to the team:

Key point:
- Donor closeout is a financial-control cycle, not just the submission of a final report.
- Final balances, assets, partner obligations, and supporting evidence must all be resolved or formally documented.

41. Q: How would you maintain financial continuity when the Finance Manager or a key accountant is absent or leaves the organization?
A: Show answer
Financial continuity requires more than having backups of files. The Finance Manager should ensure that recurring processes have documented owners, backup staff, deadlines, system access arrangements, reconciliations, and clear procedures. Critical responsibilities such as bank payments, payroll, month-end close, donor reporting, partner monitoring, and statutory filing should never depend entirely on one person's memory. Access rights should be reviewed when staff leave, and handover should include open items, unresolved reconciliations, upcoming deadlines, and key contacts. Power Query can make recurring reporting reproducible from controlled source files, while VBA can standardize close packs, checklists, and handover reports. A continuity register can show each critical finance process, primary owner, backup owner, next deadline, current status, and location of supporting files. This strengthens resilience during leave, turnover, emergency deployment, or rapid programme expansion.

42. Q: How would you design an exception-based finance review so that senior management focuses on significant risks rather than hundreds of routine transactions?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should define risk indicators that identify transactions requiring human attention, such as missing approvals, unmatched bank items, overdue advances, unsupported partner costs, unusual vendor activity, invalid project codes, large manual journals, unexpected budget variances, old reconciliations, and balances outside agreed thresholds. Routine items that satisfy established controls can move through the process efficiently, while exceptions are routed to the appropriate reviewer. Thresholds should be reviewed periodically so the system does not generate excessive false positives or overlook emerging risks. Power Query can produce centralized exception tables across multiple source files, while VBA can create management dashboards and escalation packs. Every exception should have a status, owner, action, and closure date where appropriate. This is particularly useful in a senior Finance Manager role because management attention is scarce and should be concentrated on issues that can affect compliance, liquidity, donor reporting, operational continuity, or financial integrity.

Send to the team:
- "Please resolve or formally escalate every material exception and do not mark an item cleared without evidence of the review performed."
Key point:
- Exception-based review allows automation to handle routine volume while management focuses on risk.
- A useful exception report needs ownership, action, and closure evidence.

43. Q: How would you use Power Query in a Finance Manager environment without allowing automation to replace accounting judgment?
A: Show answer
Power Query is particularly useful for repeatable data preparation: importing accounting extracts, bank files, partner reports, payroll information, budgets, grant schedules, and other structured sources; standardizing column names; transforming dates and currencies; merging tables; identifying duplicates; and producing exception datasets. It should not be treated as a substitute for deciding whether an expense is eligible, whether a control exception is acceptable, or whether a donor interpretation is correct. The Finance Manager should document source files, query logic, refresh steps, and validation checks so that the automated process remains reproducible and reviewable. Independent totals should be compared with the underlying source to confirm that the transformation has not dropped or duplicated records. Where a query identifies an anomaly, a qualified reviewer should decide what it means and what action is required.

Send to the team:
- "Please use Power Query for repeatable data preparation and exception analysis, but retain human review for accounting classification, donor interpretation, and control decisions."
Key point:
- Power Query is strongest when repetitive data preparation can be standardized.
- Automation should support professional judgment rather than conceal or replace it.

44. Q: How would you use Excel VBA responsibly for recurring finance processes such as reconciliations, close packs, and reporting?
A: Show answer
VBA is most useful where the process is repetitive and the required logic is stable, such as generating standardized reports, preparing control checklists, exporting approved reports, refreshing calculations, formatting recurring packs, or logging workflow actions. The Finance Manager should ensure that macros fail safely, validate input, protect critical outputs, and produce clear messages when required data is missing. Macro security and user access should be controlled, especially where workbooks are shared across teams. A VBA process should not silently alter accounting data without a clear audit trail and approved design. The underlying calculation logic should remain understandable enough for independent review. Where a macro generates journals or other financial outputs, the resulting batch should be reviewed under normal authorization procedures. The objective is consistent execution of routine finance processes while preserving accountability and traceability.

Send to the team:
- "Please use VBA to standardize repetitive finance workflows and logs, but require normal financial review and approval for every accounting output produced by the automation."
Key point:
- VBA is best used for controlled, repeatable workflow automation.
- Automation does not remove the need for authorization, validation, and audit evidence.

45. Q: Why should financial automation be designed around an audit trail from the beginning?
A: Show answer
A controlled automation process should make it possible to reconstruct what happened, which data was used, what transformation occurred, who initiated the process, what output was generated, and who approved the financial result. Without an audit trail, a workbook may produce a correct-looking report but provide weak evidence about how it was created. The Finance Manager should therefore define version control, source-file identification, run dates, reviewer fields, exception logs, journal references, and output storage. VBA can record workflow actions, export control reports, and write batch information into a log. Power Query can retain a controlled list of source files and refresh history. The audit trail should be proportionate to the risk; not every formatting change requires logging, but changes that create or influence accounting outputs should be traceable. This strengthens both internal review and external audit support.

Send to the team:
- "Please ensure every automated finance output can be traced back to its source data, processing batch, reviewer, and final approved output."
Key point:
- Automation without traceability can create a new control risk instead of reducing one.
- Audit trails should focus on actions that affect financial results or approvals.
Official links:
- Head of Finance — DevelopmentAid
- Impactpool vacancy — 1233919
46. Q: How would you determine whether a finance process actually needs automation?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should first map the process and identify where time, error, duplication, or control weakness occurs. A process is a strong automation candidate when the inputs are sufficiently structured, the decision rules are stable, the activity is repeated frequently, and the output can be independently checked. Bank reconciliation, data consolidation, ageing analysis, recurring management reports, partner reporting templates, and exception tracking are often suitable because much of the work is repetitive. By contrast, complex accounting judgments, unusual donor interpretations, investigations, or one-off negotiations should remain heavily review-driven. The Finance Manager should calculate the expected benefit in time saved, error reduction, control visibility, and scalability rather than automating merely because a macro or query is technically possible. Power Query is useful for repeatable transformation and consolidation; VBA is useful for workflow and output automation. The final control design should always include an exception path for cases that do not fit the standard rule.

Send to the team:
- "Please automate repetitive, rules-based work first and keep accounting judgment and unusual exceptions under explicit human review."
Key point:
- The best automation candidates are repetitive processes with stable rules and verifiable outputs.
- Process quality should be improved before automation so that a bad process is not simply made faster.

- Head of Finance — DevelopmentAid

47. Q: How would you maintain governance reporting so that senior leadership and oversight bodies receive reliable financial information without overwhelming them with transaction detail?
A: Show answer
Governance reporting should focus on material financial developments, risk, compliance, liquidity, grant performance, significant variances, audit issues, and decisions requiring oversight. The Finance Manager should establish a reporting calendar and clear definitions for key indicators so that the same measure is not calculated differently from one meeting to the next. Where exceptions are significant, management should receive the underlying cause, financial exposure, proposed action, responsible owner, and expected resolution date. Supporting schedules should remain available even if the board-level report is concise. Power Query can refresh standardized governance dashboards from the approved accounting and budget data, while VBA can prepare recurring board or senior-management packs from controlled templates. The Finance Manager should also ensure that governance reporting does not conceal uncertainty; significant estimates, unresolved reconciliations, or disputed donor balances should be identified clearly where material. This creates a transparent bridge between detailed finance operations and organizational oversight.

Send to the team:
- "Please escalate material financial risks with the amount, cause, exposure, action owner, and expected resolution date."
Key point:
- Governance reporting should emphasize materiality, risk, compliance, and decisions rather than transaction volume.
- Concise reporting is credible only when detailed supporting evidence is available behind it.

48. Q: How would you strengthen financial controls when the finance function is supporting both headquarters requirements and field-level programme delivery?
A: Show answer
The Finance Manager should establish a consistent control framework while allowing procedures to reflect the practical conditions of each location. Core requirements such as authorization, segregation of duties, bank reconciliation, budget monitoring, supporting documentation, donor compliance, and period-end review should remain consistent. Local adaptations should be documented where legal, operational, or security conditions require a different process. The Finance Manager should monitor headquarters-posted transactions, field-office advances, partner balances, cash transfers, and reporting timelines as one connected control environment. Regular communication with programme and field teams is important because weak controls often arise where operational workflows and finance procedures do not align. Power Query can bring multiple-office data into a standardized review model, while VBA can produce common checklists and exception reports across locations. A strong regional or country finance function should therefore achieve standardization of principles without forcing every office into an identical operational workflow where local conditions differ.

Send to the team:
- "Please apply the common finance-control principles consistently and document any approved local adaptation to the standard process."
Key point:
- Standardization should focus on control principles and accountability, not identical operational steps in every location.
- Headquarters and field finance should reconcile through common reporting and exception processes.

1. Q: How would you provide strategic financial leadership in an NGO while keeping finance connected to operations?
A: Show answer
Strategic financial leadership starts with turning accounting information into decisions about resources, risk, timing, and program delivery. In an NGO environment, the Finance Manager should connect the annual budget, monthly actuals, cash position, donor restrictions, grant commitments, forecasts, and operational plans rather than treating each as a separate finance exercise. The role described for Oxfam places financial management, risk management, systems, controls, budgeting, forecasting, reporting, audits, governance, donors, and partners within one leadership responsibility. The Finance Manager should therefore establish a management rhythm in which significant financial movements are reviewed with budget holders, risks are escalated early, and operational decisions are supported by reliable financial information. Power Query can consolidate approved accounting exports, grant schedules, budgets, and cash information into a common model, while VBA can prepare recurring management packs and exception registers. The control objective is not to automate judgment but to make reliable information available early enough for management to act.

Send to the team:
- "Please connect financial reporting, cash, budgets, grants, risks, and operational decisions in one monthly management review."
Key point:
- Strategic finance links accounting information to operational decisions.
- A single management rhythm helps identify financial risks before they become reporting or delivery problems.

2. Q: How would you establish an effective financial-control framework across an NGO office?
A: Show answer
A control framework should begin by identifying the significant financial risks and then assigning preventive and detective controls to those risks. For an NGO Finance Manager, this includes documented policies, approval authorities, reconciliations, reporting reviews, segregation of duties, secure records, audit trails, and periodic testing of whether controls actually operate as designed. The Oxfam role specifically emphasizes having appropriate policies, systems, controls, and ways of working in place, documented, understood, complied with, reviewed, and improved. That requires more than writing procedures. The Finance Manager should map each major process from initiation through posting, payment, reporting, and archival, identify who performs and reviews each stage, and define evidence of completion. Power Query can help test large transaction populations for unusual patterns or missing fields, while VBA can maintain control checklists and flag incomplete reviews. The resulting framework should also distinguish between a control that exists on paper and one that is consistently evidenced in practice.

Send to the team:
- "Please map every major finance process to a documented control, responsible person, reviewer, and retained evidence."
Key point:
- Effective controls need ownership, evidence, and review.
- Finance should test operating effectiveness rather than relying only on written procedures.
Official links:
- Finance Manager (INT11828) — Oxfam
3. Q: How would you identify and reduce fraud, wastage, and leakage risks in NGO finance?
A: Show answer
Fraud, wastage, and leakage require a structured risk-based approach. The Finance Manager should identify where money can be diverted, where approvals can be bypassed, where duplicate or unsupported transactions can enter the system, and where weak monitoring could allow losses to remain undetected. Controls should then be prioritized according to exposure and likelihood. Examples include segregation of duties, delegated authority, transaction review, bank controls, vendor and partner checks, exception reporting, secure record keeping, and independent review of unusual transactions. Oxfam’s Finance Manager role explicitly requires proactive assessment of controls and introduction of measures to reduce fraud, wastage, and leakage, as well as leadership in anti-fraud and corruption efforts. Power Query can identify duplicate references, repeated bank amounts, unusual timing, and transactions outside expected categories, while VBA can generate exception logs for investigation. The Finance Manager must still investigate the underlying evidence and document conclusions rather than treating an automated flag as proof of misconduct.

Send to the team:
- "Please review fraud and leakage indicators through documented exceptions and investigate unusual transactions before closing the period."
Key point:
- Automated flags identify exceptions but do not replace investigation.
- Fraud prevention depends on both preventive controls and effective follow-up.
Official links:
- Finance Manager (INT11828) — Oxfam
4. Q: How would you ensure compliance with organizational, legal, and statutory reporting requirements?
A: Show answer
Compliance should be managed as a calendar of obligations rather than as a last-minute reporting exercise. The Finance Manager should identify each recurring organizational, legal, tax, regulatory, donor, and year-end requirement, assign responsibility, define review deadlines, and retain evidence of submission and approval. The Oxfam role requires monitoring financial controls and compliance with organizational, legal, and statutory reporting requirements and ensuring annual budgets and year-end returns are submitted in line with applicable requirements and standards. A compliance matrix can therefore track requirement, period, preparer, reviewer, due date, status, supporting evidence, and escalation point. Power Query can consolidate reporting status across multiple schedules, while VBA can produce deadline dashboards and overdue reminders. The key is to keep accounting records and reporting evidence aligned so that a submitted figure can be traced back to its supporting ledger, reconciliation, grant information, or tax documentation.

Send to the team:
- "Please maintain one compliance calendar covering statutory, organizational, donor, and year-end reporting obligations."
Key point:
- Compliance should be scheduled and evidenced.
- A complete audit trail links submissions back to underlying records.

5. Q: How would you plan and control an NGO annual budgeting process?
A: Show answer
Annual budgeting should start from approved strategic and program plans and translate them into realistic income, expenditure, staffing, grant, and cash assumptions. The Finance Manager should establish the timetable, issue consistent templates, review assumptions with budget holders, challenge unsupported increases or decreases, check donor restrictions, and ensure the final budget can be reconciled to the organization's financial structure. Because the Oxfam role includes annual budgeting as well as monthly, quarterly, and year-end processes, the budget should not be treated as a document prepared once a year. It should become the baseline for monthly monitoring and forecasting. Power Query can combine approved budget submissions into a standard model, while VBA can validate required fields and produce consolidation schedules. The Finance Manager should retain evidence of assumptions, approvals, revisions, and version history so that later budget changes can be explained rather than appearing as unexplained movements.

Send to the team:
- "Please prepare budgets from documented operational assumptions and retain approvals for all material revisions."
Key point:
- An annual budget should connect strategic plans to measurable financial assumptions.
- Budget versions and changes should remain traceable throughout the year.

6. Q: How would you manage monthly, quarterly, and year-end financial closing as one controlled process?
A: Show answer
A strong close process uses the same core control architecture at different reporting frequencies. The Finance Manager should establish a close calendar covering ledger completion, bank reconciliations, balance-sheet reviews, accruals and prepayments where applicable, grant and donor schedules, variance analysis, reporting review, and final submission. The Oxfam vacancy explicitly combines monthly, quarterly, and year-end processes, so the close should be designed as a repeatable sequence rather than a collection of disconnected deadlines. Power Query can refresh recurring schedules from approved accounting exports, while VBA can update close checklists and highlight incomplete tasks. The Finance Manager should also distinguish normal timing differences from unresolved accounting issues. A balance that is carried forward must have a clear explanation, ownership, expected resolution date, and supporting evidence. Year-end should add additional review rather than simply repeating the month-end process at greater volume.

Send to the team:
- "Please use one close checklist with separate monthly, quarterly, and year-end review requirements."
Key point:
- A standardized close process improves consistency across reporting periods.
- Outstanding balances should have documented owners and resolution dates.

7. Q: How would you manage cash flow and expenditure so that program delivery is protected without weakening financial control?
A: Show answer
Cash management requires a forward-looking view of expected receipts, committed payments, payroll, operational expenditure, grants, and other material outflows. The Finance Manager should maintain a rolling cash-flow forecast, compare forecast to actual movement, identify periods of pressure, and ensure that planned expenditure is supported by available funding and delegated approvals. The Oxfam role specifically requires proper cash-flow and expenditure management and control. Cash should therefore be managed alongside grant and budget information rather than as a bank balance alone. Power Query can consolidate bank balances, payment forecasts, approved budgets, and expected funding receipts into a cash dashboard, while VBA can produce weekly or monthly exception reports against forecast. The Finance Manager should investigate material forecast variances and distinguish genuine liquidity pressure from delays in coding, reporting, or documentation. This supports continuity of operations while maintaining disciplined use of restricted and unrestricted resources.

Send to the team:
- "Please update the rolling cash forecast with approved commitments and investigate material changes against the prior forecast."
Key point:
- Liquidity management must reflect expected receipts and committed expenditure.
- Cash availability should never be treated as unrestricted spending authority.

- Finance Manager (INT11828) — Oxfam
8. Q: How would you use budget-versus-actual analysis to support budget holders?
A: Show answer
Budget-versus-actual analysis should move beyond reporting a numerical variance. The Finance Manager should determine whether a variance comes from timing, volume, price, coding, procurement, implementation delays, funding restrictions, or an unreliable original forecast. The Oxfam role requires periodic review of actual-versus-budget variances, trend analysis, identification of concerns and risks, follow-up with budget holders, and financial management advice on resource utilization and forecasts. A good monthly review therefore identifies material variances, asks the responsible manager for an explanation, evaluates whether corrective action is required, and updates the forecast where assumptions have changed. Power Query can automate consolidation by project, donor, department, and account, while VBA can produce variance packs with predefined thresholds. The Finance Manager should retain the explanation and decision, not just the number, so that future reviews can distinguish recurring issues from one-off events.

Send to the team:
- "Please provide an explanation and corrective action for every material budget variance before the monthly review is finalized."
Key point:
- Variance analysis should explain causes and actions, not simply differences.
- Finance should use variance trends to improve forecasting and management decisions.

9. Q: How would you improve forecasting when actual spending indicates that the approved budget is no longer realistic?
A: Show answer
Forecasting should be based on the latest operational information, not on the assumption that the original budget will remain correct. The Finance Manager should review actual expenditure, commitments, implementation timing, staffing assumptions, expected donor income, grant balances, and known changes to activities. Where those conditions have changed, the forecast should be updated and the reason for the change documented. This is especially important where the Oxfam role requires advice to budget holders about underlying forecasts. A forecast should show the expected final position and the assumptions supporting it, not simply spread the remaining budget evenly across remaining months. Power Query can combine actuals, commitments, and approved forecast inputs, while VBA can produce a rolling forecast workbook and highlight movements between forecast versions. Management should challenge major changes and consider their impact on cash, donor reporting, and resource allocation.

Send to the team:
- "Please revise forecasts when operating assumptions change and document the reason for every material movement."
Key point:
- Forecasts should reflect current operational reality.
- Forecast changes need documented assumptions and management review.

10. Q: How would you review balance-sheet reconciliations and investigate unusual asset and liability movements?
A: Show answer
A balance-sheet reconciliation should explain the closing balance, not merely prove that two numbers agree. The Finance Manager should review bank, receivable, payable, restricted-income debtor, accrual, prepayment, and other material balances according to the organization's accounting structure, while focusing on significant or extraordinary movements. The Oxfam role specifically requires review and verification of monthly and quarterly account returns and balance-sheet reconciliations and appropriate action for significant movements in asset and liability accounts. A controlled reconciliation should show opening balance, movements, closing balance, supporting detail, reconciling items, age, ownership, and expected resolution. Power Query can compare subledger or supporting schedules to general-ledger exports, while VBA can flag old reconciling items and generate a management exception list. The Finance Manager should challenge balances that remain unexplained or simply roll forward each period.

Send to the team:

Key point:
- Reconciliations explain the balance, not just the arithmetic.

12. Q: Why should restricted contract balances be reconciled separately from general financial reporting?
A: Show answer
A restricted contract balance represents a funding relationship with conditions that may differ from the organization's overall accounting balances. The Finance Manager should reconcile opening restricted balance, income recognized or available, eligible expenditure, adjustments, transfers where permitted, and closing restricted balance against the donor or contract record. Oxfam specifically requires review and verification of restricted contract balances and prompt resolution of issues arising from those reconciliations. The schedule should therefore identify the contract, funding period, restrictions, financial movements, remaining balance, and any items requiring donor clarification. Power Query can combine contract schedules with accounting transactions and classify movements by grant or project code, while VBA can create recurring restricted-balance reports and highlight unusual changes. This helps prevent unsupported expenditure from being masked by overall organizational totals.

Send to the team:
- "Please maintain a separate reconciliation for each material restricted contract and resolve differences promptly."
Key point:
- Restricted balances require contract-level visibility.
- Grant-level reconciliation reduces the risk of reporting expenditure against the wrong funding conditions.

13. Q: How would you manage restricted-income debtor balances and prevent them from remaining unresolved?
A: Show answer
A restricted-income debtor should be treated as an active receivable requiring evidence, aging, and follow-up. The Finance Manager should verify the underlying claim, approved funding, amount due, expected receipt timing, and any conditions that could delay collection. Oxfam’s role requires proactive action to clear restricted-income debtor balances when due. A debtor schedule should therefore include donor or contract reference, date recognized, amount, due date, collection status, correspondence, and escalation route. Power Query can compare the debtor schedule with general-ledger balances and bank receipts, while VBA can age balances and prepare follow-up lists. Management should pay particular attention to aged balances because they can indicate incorrect recognition, delayed claims, disputed costs, or liquidity pressure.

Send to the team:
- "Please review all restricted-income debtor balances by age and document the collection or resolution action for each material item."
Key point:
- A debtor is not cleared simply because a claim was submitted.
- Aging and follow-up help distinguish collectible balances from unresolved accounting issues.

33. Q: How would you investigate and resolve payroll discrepancies?
A: Show answer
A payroll discrepancy should be traced back to the originating data and then through the calculation and payment process. The Payroll Manager should determine whether the issue arose from employee master data, a change in employment information, an incorrect calculation, a processing error, or a communication gap with HR. The response should document the issue, impact, correction, approval, and communication to affected parties. Where financial statements are affected, the corresponding accounting correction should also be identified. Power Query can compare current and prior payroll populations to highlight unusual changes, while VBA can create discrepancy registers and track resolution status. The key is to distinguish legitimate employee changes from unexpected payroll movements so that control alerts produce useful investigations rather than excessive false positives.

Send to the team:
- "Please document the cause, impact, correction, approval, and closure evidence for every material payroll discrepancy."
Key point:
- Payroll corrections should address both the immediate error and its root cause.
- Discrepancy logs help identify repeated process weaknesses.

53. Q: How would you distinguish a genuine financial risk from a temporary reporting difference?
A: Show answer
Not every variance is a control failure. A Finance Manager should examine timing, materiality, underlying transaction evidence, operational context, and whether the difference will self-clear through an expected future transaction. For example, a restricted debtor that is due and supported may be a timing matter, whereas an old unsupported debtor is a deeper control concern. A payroll difference caused by a documented employee change is different from an unexplained payment variance. Power Query can classify and age exceptions using repeatable rules, while VBA can prepare a review queue. The Finance Manager should then assess whether the item requires correction, monitoring, escalation, or no further action, with the conclusion documented.

Send to the team:
- "Please classify material exceptions by cause, expected resolution, financial impact, and control risk before escalating them."
Key point:
- Materiality and cause matter when assessing financial risk.
- Documented classification improves consistency in management escalation.

1. Q: How would you manage accounting and reporting when financial operations cover two legal entities through one shared services structure?
A: The key control is to preserve the legal-entity identity of every transaction while standardizing the operating process. The Finance Manager should establish separate ledgers, bank accounts, statutory obligations, budgets and reporting outputs for each entity, while using common procedures, review checklists, approval matrices and service standards within the shared services unit. Inter-entity transactions should have clear counterpart references and reconciliation procedures so that balances agree in both entities. Monthly close should include entity-level reconciliations first, followed by a shared-services review of exceptions and cross-entity balances. Management reports can then show each entity separately and, where appropriate, combined operational information without obscuring statutory boundaries. A control matrix should identify which accounting activities are centralized and which remain legally accountable to each entity. Power Query can consolidate transaction extracts while retaining a mandatory Legal Entity field, and VBA can validate that each journal contains the correct entity and reference before a reporting pack is generated.
Send to the team:
- “Please preserve legal-entity coding in every shared-services transaction and reconcile all inter-entity balances before month-end close.”
Key point:
- Shared services can standardize processes without merging legal-entity accountability.
- Entity-level ledgers, reconciliations and statutory reporting must remain controlled.

2. Q: Why is monthly close discipline important when a Finance Manager is responsible for key account reconciliations and financial reporting?
A: Month-end close is the point at which the Finance Manager tests whether operational transactions have been converted into a reliable financial position. The close should not be treated as simply running a report. It should include completion of bank, payroll, receivable, payable, accrual, clearing and other significant balance-sheet reconciliations, review of unusual movements, investigation of aged or recurring items, and confirmation that budget and actual information is complete. Where the role supports more than one organization, the close timetable should specify entity-level deadlines and reviewer responsibilities. Evidence should include reconciliations, supporting schedules, explanations of material movements and documentation of outstanding exceptions. A strong close process also links accounting results to management reporting so that unexplained balances do not become accepted simply because the ledger has been closed. Power Query can consolidate reconciliation statuses and identify accounts without current evidence, while VBA can generate the month-end checklist and escalate incomplete reconciliations to the responsible owner.
Send to the team:
- “Please complete and evidence every key account reconciliation before the monthly financial report is finalized.”
Key point:
- Close quality depends on reconciliations, review and documented resolution of exceptions.
- Financial reports should be supported by an auditable close file.

3. Q: How would you review budget performance and explain variances to budget holders and senior management?
A: The Finance Manager should distinguish between numerical variance and the underlying operational driver. The review should start with approved budget, actual expenditure, commitments where available, forecast and year-to-date position. Significant variances should then be classified by cause, such as timing, volume, staffing, procurement changes, changed activity plans or one-off items. A budget holder should receive a clear explanation of what happened, whether the variance is temporary or structural, and what action is required. Where savings are identified, the Finance Manager should assess whether they are genuine recurring savings, delayed expenditure or idle funds that may need planned reallocation. In the shared-services environment, the same methodology should be used consistently across the two entities. Evidence should include variance schedules, management comments, revised forecasts and approved actions. Power Query can combine budget and ledger extracts and standardize variance calculations, while VBA can produce a monthly exception report showing the largest unfavorable and favorable movements for management review.
Send to the team:
- “Please explain material budget variances by cause, forecast impact and required management action rather than reporting percentages alone.”
Key point:
- Variance analysis is a management tool, not just a reporting calculation.
- Savings and underspending should be distinguished from genuinely available funds.

- Senior Management Accountant — Office of the Public Guardian
- ASSISTANT MANAGER ACCOUNTING - MANILA — Children International
4. Q: How would you build an annual budgeting process that works for both an operating finance function and multiple departments or legal entities?
A: The Finance Manager should establish a documented budgeting timetable, assumptions, responsibilities, submission format and approval path. Department heads should understand what information is required, while Finance should review submissions for arithmetic accuracy, consistency with organizational plans, staffing implications, recurring commitments and realistic expenditure timing. Where two legal entities are supported, each should maintain its own approved budget, while the shared-services unit should have its own work-plan and operating budget. Forecasting should then be linked to the approved budget rather than treated as a separate exercise. After approval, changes should be controlled through documented budget amendments with a clear audit trail. Management should also receive regular budget-monitoring reports and recommendations on resource utilization. Power Query can standardize departmental submissions into one controlled model while preserving entity and department dimensions, and VBA can check for missing assumptions, duplicate lines and unresolved budget-review comments before consolidation.
Send to the team:
- “Please submit departmental budgets using the approved template and document all revisions through the formal budget-change process.”
Key point:
- Budgeting requires governance, assumptions, ownership and controlled revisions.
- Separate entity budgets should remain traceable even when prepared through shared services.

5. Q: How would you prepare and quality-assure a recurring workforce or FTE financial report?
A: A workforce report should connect headcount or FTE data to the financial resources available to support those positions. The Finance Manager should establish a controlled source for approved positions, current employees, funding availability and authorized changes. The report should be reconciled to payroll and the approved budget, with differences investigated before the report is distributed. Where funding and FTE availability are explicitly monitored, budget changes should be supported by approval evidence and reflected consistently in the underlying financial model. The Finance Manager should also look for structural issues such as positions being budgeted but unfunded, funded positions remaining vacant, or staffing changes occurring without corresponding budget action. This information is useful not only to Finance but also to operational managers making resource decisions. Power Query can combine HR, payroll and budget extracts, while VBA can flag missing employee identifiers, unapproved changes and mismatches between approved FTE and current payroll data.
Send to the team:
- “Please reconcile FTE, payroll and approved funding before issuing the workforce report and document all approved changes.”
Key point:
- FTE reporting should connect people data with funding and budget control.
- Workforce changes need evidence and financial authorization.

6. Q: Why should payroll review be treated as a finance-control process rather than only a payroll administration task?
A: Payroll affects expenditure, liabilities, statutory compliance and cash flow simultaneously. A Finance Manager should ensure that payroll is reviewed against approved employee records, pay rates, authorized changes, funding and appropriate statutory deductions before payment and posting. Reconciliations should compare payroll reports to the general ledger and investigate unusual movements, terminated employees, duplicate payments and unexpected cost-center changes. Where payroll processing supports two legal entities, entity ownership and statutory obligations must remain clear. The review should leave evidence of who prepared, checked and approved the payroll and what exceptions were resolved. Finance should also monitor whether payroll-related costs are consistent with budget and forecast assumptions. Power Query can compare payroll output with employee master data and budget files, while VBA can generate a payroll review checklist and exception summary for the Finance Manager.
Send to the team:
- “Please reconcile every payroll run to approved employee data, the general ledger and the relevant entity budget before final approval.”
Key point:
- Payroll is a financial, compliance and cash-control process.
- Payroll reconciliations should be completed and reviewed before close.

7. Q: How would you control procurement from the initial request through purchase order and contract management?
A: The Finance Manager should treat procurement as an integrated financial-control process rather than a separate purchasing activity. The control sequence should connect approved need, budget availability, procurement method, delegated authority, procurement documentation, purchase order, contract terms, receipt of goods or services, invoice verification and payment. In the OPG role, this includes compliance with the Queensland Procurement Policy, procurement delegations, procurement memos, purchase orders and contract management. A finance review should confirm that expenditure remains within approval limits and that commitments are captured consistently for budget monitoring. Contract registers should identify supplier, value, term, owner, renewal date and financial obligations. For an NGO environment, the same control logic can also be applied to donor-funded procurement, with donor-specific requirements added where applicable. Power Query can combine procurement and accounting exports for commitment monitoring, while VBA can flag missing purchase orders or contracts before payment processing.
Send to the team:
- “Please confirm budget availability, procurement authority, required documentation and purchase-order status before processing supplier commitments.”
Key point:
- Procurement controls should connect authorization, budget, contract and payment.
- Finance should monitor the full transaction lifecycle, not only the invoice stage.

8. Q: How would you review a supplier invoice where procurement approval, purchase order, contract terms and payment processing are handled by different teams?
A: The Finance Manager should define a clear three-way or equivalent control between the approved commitment, evidence that the goods or services were received, and the invoice. Where responsibilities are distributed among Procurement, Programs, Operations and Finance, the workflow should identify who owns each control. Finance should not assume that an invoice is valid merely because another team has requested payment. The review should confirm supplier identity, purchase-order or contract reference, authorization, amount, applicable tax treatment and accounting classification. Where Queensland Shared Services handles transactional processing, Finance still needs oversight of the upstream control environment and exception handling. In an NGO setting, the equivalent process should also verify donor eligibility where the expenditure is grant-funded. Automation can compare invoice data with purchase-order and vendor tables, but approval decisions and unusual exceptions should remain with authorized staff.
Send to the team:
- “Please do not release an invoice solely on the basis of a payment request; confirm authorization, supporting evidence and accounting treatment.”
Key point:
- Transaction processing can be centralized without transferring accountability for upstream controls.
- Exceptions must be reviewed rather than forced through an automated match.
